\displaystyle V^{[2],\rho,T}_{t}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{1:2}f_{t}^{[2],\rho,T}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\psi(\xi_{1})\psi(\xi_{2}).

If
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})
is charged, then
\kappa_{t}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})=1
and it follows
from
|\psi(\xi)|=1
combined with (
3.28
) from Lemma

3.6
,

|V^{[\ell],\rho,T}_{t}(\varphi)|\lesssim\interleave f^{[2]}_{t}\interleave_{t}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1}.

If
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})
is neutral, we have by a Taylor expansion

\psi(\xi_{1})\psi(\xi_{2})=1+\psi(\xi_{1})(x_{2}-x_{1})\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\nabla_{x}\psi(x_{1}+\vartheta(x_{2}-x_{1})).

(3.34)

Therefore, choosing

c^{[2],\rho,T}:=\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{1:2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\partial_{s}{f}_{s}^{[2](0),\rho,T}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2}),

(3.35)

it follows that

\displaystyle V^{[2](0),\rho,T}_{T}(\varphi)=

\displaystyle\int_{(\mdmathbb{R}^{2})^{2}}\mathrm{d}x_{1:2}(\kappa_{T}f_{T}^{[2](0),\rho,T})(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\kappa_{T}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})^{-1}(x_{2}-x_{1})\psi(\xi_{1})\times

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\nabla_{x}\psi(x_{1}+\vartheta(x_{2}-x_{1}))+c^{[2],\rho,T},

and thus,

\displaystyle|V_{T}^{[2](0),\rho,T}(\varphi)-c^{[2],\rho.T}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}|\kappa_{T}^{-1}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})(x_{1}-x_{2})|\interleave f_{T}^{[2],\rho,T}\interleave_{T}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}

(3.36)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\rho}\bar{\lambda}_{T}^{2}\langle T\rangle^{-1}\left(\left\langle T\right\rangle^{-1/2}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\right).

We summarise the bounds in the following Lemma for later reference.

Lemma 3.9
.

For
\ell^{\ast}=3
, there is a choice for

c^{[\ell],\rho,T}
such that for any
\rho\preceq 1
, and
T<\infty
,

\displaystyle|V_{T}^{\rho,T}(\varphi)|\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\rho\|_{L^{1}}\sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}[]{c}\ell\leqslant\ell^{\ast}\\
n\leqslant\lfloor{\ell/2}\rfloor\end{array}$}}}\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{\ell}\langle T\rangle^{-(\ell-1)}\left(1+\left\langle T\right\rangle^{-1/2}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)^{n},

(3.37)

\displaystyle\|F_{t}^{\rho,T}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}[]{c}\ell\leqslant\ell^{\ast}\\
n\leqslant\lfloor{\ell/2}\rfloor\end{array}$}}}\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{\ell}\langle t\rangle^{-(\ell-1)}\left(1+\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-1/2}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)^{n}.

Proof.

The estimate on
V
follows directly by inserting the bound (
3.36
) and the estimates (
3.21
) on the coefficients in (
3.33
).
In the same way, we can start from

\|F^{[\ell]}_{t}(\varphi)\|\lesssim\sup_{x}|\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{1:\ell}f^{[\ell]}_{t}(\xi_{1:\ell})\big(\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\sigma_{j}\delta_{x}(x_{j})\big)\psi(\xi_{1:\ell})|,

which with (
3.21
) yields the desired estimate for all terms except for
\ell=2,q=0
. For
F^{[2](0)}
, we follow the same steps as for
V^{[2](0)}
, with the important distinction that the constant term
absorbed by (
3.35
) vanishes since
f(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})=f(\xi_{2},\xi_{1})
which implies

\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{1:\ell}\sigma_{1}f_{t}^{[2](0)}(\xi_{1:2})\big(\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\sigma_{j}\delta_{x}(x_{j})\big)=0.

∎

Remark 3.10
.

If the order of the approximation
\ell^{\ast}
and the
smoothing
\alpha\in(0,1)
is chosen appropriately (
\alpha>1-2\delta
, according to (
3.11
) and Lemma

3.1
), then by modifying (
3.34
),
one can show that these estimates generalise in the full subcritical regime

\beta^{2}<8\pi
and
0<t<T\leqslant\infty
to bounds of the form

\displaystyle|V_{T}^{\rho,T}(\varphi)|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\rho}\sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}[]{c}\ell\leqslant\ell^{\ast}\\
n\leqslant\lfloor{\ell/2}\rfloor\end{array}$}}}\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{\ell}\langle T\rangle^{-(\ell-1)}\left(1+\left\langle T\right\rangle^{-\alpha}\|\varphi\|_{B_{\infty,\infty}^{2\alpha}}\right)^{n},

\displaystyle\quad\rho\prec 1,

(3.38)

\displaystyle\|F_{t}^{\rho,T}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}[]{c}\ell\leqslant\ell^{\ast}\\
n\leqslant\lfloor{\ell/2}\rfloor\end{array}$}}}\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{\ell}\langle t\rangle^{-(\ell-1)}\left(1+\left\langle t\right\rangle^{-\alpha}\|\varphi\|_{B_{\infty,\infty}^{2\alpha}}\right)^{n},

\displaystyle\quad\rho\leqslant 1.

However, the field dependency in the estimates (
3.37
) and
(
3.38
) means that we are currently not able to control the
FBSDE uniformly in the UV-cut-off which restricts our analysis to the regime

\beta^{2}<6\pi
. What saves our analysis in this case is the observation
that for the FBSDEs (
2.5
) and (
2.27
) the
force
F_{s}
only appears in combination with the heat kernel. Indeed, it
turns out that the smoothing
properties of the heat kernel are enough to recover field independent bounds for
Q_{s}F_{s}
and
\mathrm{D}F_{s}Q_{s}
.

Remark 3.11
.

The truncated solutions still satisfy for any function
\varphi
and any
T<\infty
,

V^{\rho,T}_{T}(\varphi)=\int_{\mdmathbb{R}^{2}}(\lambda_{T}\cos(\beta\varphi)-c^{\rho,T})\rho(x)\mathrm{d}x,\quad F^{\rho,T}_{t}(\varphi)(x)=-\beta\lambda_{T}\rho(x)\sin(\beta\varphi(x)).

(3.39)

Here, the renormalisation constant
\lambda_{t}=\lambda\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}G_{t}(0)}
is the usual Wick-ordering and
c^{\rho,T}:=\sum_{\ell}c^{[\ell],\rho,T}
is the additive renormalisation
resulting from higher order corrections.

3.5
Estimates on the force

From now on we will always assume that
\beta^{2}<6\pi
and that in the
definition (
3.25
) of the kernel
\kappa_{t}
we fix
c\in(0,1/4)

sufficiently close to
1/4
and
\alpha=1/2
. Since we only deal with
the case
\ell^{\ast}=3
, let us also agree to suppress the dependence on

\ell^{\ast}
for
V,F
and
H
writing e.g.
F:=F^{[\leqslant\ell^{\ast}]}=F^{[\leqslant 3]}
. Our goal in this section is to recover
field independent bounds on all coefficients of (
4.1
), that is
on
Q_{s}F_{s}
,
\mathrm{D}F_{s}Q_{s}
and
H_{s}
.

Lemma 3.12
.

For any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mdmathbb{R}^{2})
,

\|Q_{t}F_{t}^{[2](0)}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim(\bar{\lambda}_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-1})^{2}.

Proof.

We follow exactly the same strategy as in the proof of
Lemma
3.7
, where we now require bounds on

\sup_{x\in\mdmathbb{R}^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}|\kappa_{t}f_{t}^{[2](0)}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})|\frac{|Q_{t}(x_{1}-x)-Q_{t}(x_{2}-x)|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|}\mathrm{e}^{-ct|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{2}}.

(3.40)

Thanks to the translation invariance, we can apply
Lemma
A.3
for
Q_{t}
and absorb the increment
|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{-1}
,

\frac{|Q_{t}(x_{1}-x)-Q_{t}(x_{2}-x)|}{|x_{1}-x_{2}|}\mathrm{e}^{-ct|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{2}}\lesssim t(|x_{1}|+|x_{1}-x_{2}|)\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{c}{2}t|x_{1}|^{2}}\mathrm{e}^{-m^{2}/2s}.

(3.41)

Using this in (
3.40
) we get from Young’s convolution inequality and
the scaling properties of the kernels
{f}^{[2]}
(see
Lemma
3.6
),

\displaystyle\|Q_{t}F_{t}^{[2](0)}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}(|x_{1}|+|x_{1}-x_{2}|)t^{-1/2}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{c}{2}t|x_{1}|^{2}-m^{2}/2t}|\kappa_{t}f_{t}^{[2](0)}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{1/2}\int\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{c}{2}t|x_{1}|^{2}-m^{2}/2t}\sup_{\xi_{1}}\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}|x_{1}-x_{2}||\kappa_{t}f_{t}^{[2](0)}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})|

\displaystyle+t^{1/2}\int\mathrm{d}x_{1}|x_{1}|\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{c}{2}t|x_{1}|^{2}-m^{2}/2t}\sup_{\xi_{1}}\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}|\kappa_{t}f_{t}^{[2](0)}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-2}.

∎

For the remaining levels, the estimates on the coefficients transfer directly
to the force. To remove the cut-offs later, we will also have to control the
dependence of the approximate solution
F
on these parameters. Therefore, let
us again keep track of this dependence by writing
F^{T}
for the solution to
the flow equation on
[0,T]
with terminal
conditions (
3.3
) at
T
and in the same way

F^{\rho}
for
\rho\preceq 1
. In the estimates it is assumed that the
suppressed parameters coincide.

Proposition 3.13
.

E=L^{\infty}
or
E=L^{2,k}
for any
k\in\mdmathbb{Z}
. For any
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mdmathbb{R}^{2})
,
R\in L^{\infty}(\mdmathbb{R}^{2})
,
\rho,\rho_{1},\rho_{2}\leqslant 1
and
T,T_{1},T_{2}<\infty
, the following estimates apply.

a)

(Uniform boundedness)
For any
p\in[1,\infty]
,

\displaystyle\|Q_{t}F_{t}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-1},

(3.42)

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{D}F_{t}(\varphi)Q_{t}R\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-1}\|R\|_{L^{\infty}},

\displaystyle\|H_{t}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\bar{\lambda}_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-1})^{4}.

b)

(Uniform Lipschitz condition)

\displaystyle\|Q_{t}F_{t}(\varphi)-Q_{t}F_{t}(\tilde{\varphi})\|_{E}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-1}(\|\varphi-\tilde{\varphi}\|_{E}),

(3.43)

\displaystyle\|(\mathrm{D}F_{t}(\varphi)Q_{t}-\mathrm{D}F_{t}(\tilde{\varphi})Q_{t})R\|_{E}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-1}(\|\varphi-\tilde{\varphi}\|_{E}\|R\|_{L^{\infty}}),

\displaystyle\|H_{t}(\varphi)-H_{t}(\tilde{\varphi})\|_{E}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\bar{\lambda}_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-1})^{4}\|\varphi-\tilde{\varphi}\|_{E}

c)

(Dependence on
T
)
There is
an
\varepsilon>0
depending only on
\beta^{2}
such that,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{-1}\langle t\rangle^{1-2\varepsilon}\|Q_{t}(F^{T_{1}}_{t}-F_{t}^{T_{2}})(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}\langle T_{1}\wedge T_{2}\rangle^{-\varepsilon},

(3.44)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{-1}\langle t\rangle^{1-2\varepsilon}\|(\mathrm{D}F^{T_{1}}_{t}-\mathrm{D}F_{t}^{T_{2}})(\varphi)Q_{t}R\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}\langle T_{1}\wedge T_{2}\rangle^{-\varepsilon}\|R\|_{L^{\infty}},

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{-1}\langle t\rangle^{1-2\varepsilon}\|H^{T_{1}}_{t}-H_{t}^{T_{2}}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}^{4}\langle T_{1}\wedge T_{2}\rangle^{-\varepsilon}.

d)

(Dependence on
\rho
)
For
any
n>2
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|Q_{t}(F^{\rho_{1}}_{t}-F_{t}^{\rho_{2}})(\varphi)\|_{L^{2,-n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-1}\|\rho_{1}-\rho_{2}\|_{L^{2,-n}},

(3.45)

\displaystyle\|(\mathrm{D}F^{\rho_{1}}_{t}-\mathrm{D}F_{t}^{\rho_{2}})(\varphi)Q_{t}R\|_{L^{2,-n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-1}\|\rho_{1}-\rho_{2}\|_{L^{2,-n}}\|R\|_{L^{\infty}},

\displaystyle\|(H^{\rho_{1}}_{t}-H_{t}^{\rho_{2}})(\varphi)\|_{L^{2,-n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\bar{\lambda}_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-1})^{4}\|\rho_{1}-\rho_{2}\|_{L^{2,-n}}.

In particular, for
E=L^{\infty}
or
E=L^{2,-n}
we obtain estimates that are uniform in the volume cut-off
\rho
.

Proof.

a)

For all contributions other than for
F^{[2](0)}
this follows
directly from the bounds on the kernels
f
and
|\psi(\xi_{1:\ell})|\leqslant 1
. In fact, in these cases, we obtain the better bound,

\|F_{t}^{[\ell]}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{\ell}\langle t\rangle^{-(\ell-1)},

so that

\|Q_{t}F_{t}^{[\ell]}(\varphi)R\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant\|Q_{t}\|_{L^{1}}\|F^{[\ell]}_{t}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|R\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{\ell}\langle t\rangle^{-(\ell-1)}\|R\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}.

For
F^{[2](0)}_{t}
, this bound was shown in
Lemma
3.12
. For the derivative, note that for any
test function
R\in L^{\infty}(\mdmathbb{R}^{2})

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{D}F^{[\ell]}_{t}(\varphi)Q_{t}R\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\xi_{1}}\left|\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{2:\ell}\int\mathrm{d}yf^{[\ell]}_{t}(\xi_{1:\ell})\sum_{k\leqslant\ell}i\beta\sigma_{k}Q_{t}(x_{k}-y)R(y)\psi(\xi_{1:\ell})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|R\|_{L^{\infty}}\sup_{\xi_{1}}\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{2:\ell}\int\mathrm{d}y\left|f^{[\ell]}_{t}(\xi_{1:\ell})\sum_{k\leqslant\ell}i\beta\sigma_{k}Q_{t}(x_{k}-y)\right|,

and now the same reasoning as for
F_{t}
applies for the integral on the
right-hand side.

Finally, the estimates on
H
follow from the estimates above and

\displaystyle\|H_{t}(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime\prime}>3}\|\mathrm{D}(F^{[\ell]}_{t}\dot{G}_{t}F^{[\ell^{\prime\prime}]}_{t})(\varphi)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\ell^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime\prime}>3}\|\mathrm{D}F_{t}^{[\ell^{\prime}]}Q_{t}(Q_{t}F^{[\ell^{\prime\prime}]}_{t})\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\sum_{\ell^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime\prime}>3}(\bar{\lambda}_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-1})^{\ell^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime\prime}}.

b)

The Lipschitz bounds follow as above in part
a
,
combined with the observation that thanks to the boundedness of the
complex exponential fields it holds that, writing
\tilde{\psi}(\xi)=\tilde{\psi}(\sigma,x)=\mathrm{e}^{i\sigma\beta\tilde{\varphi}(x)}
,

|\psi(\xi_{1:\ell})-\tilde{\psi}(\xi_{1:\ell})|\leqslant\sum_{k}|\psi(\xi_{k})-\tilde{\psi}(\xi_{k})|\leqslant\sum_{k}|\varphi(\xi_{k})-\tilde{\varphi}(\xi_{k})|.

c)

This follows from Lemma
B.1
in the same way
as Proposition
4.5
-
a
followed from
Lemma
3.6
and
3.7
.

d)

We only show the estimates on
QF
as the others are a direct
consequence as illustrated in the proof of part
a
.
Again, except for
F^{[2](0)}_{t}
, these estimates follow immediately from
the convolution inequalities (see Lemma
A.4
),

\displaystyle\|Q_{t}(F_{t}^{[\ell],\rho_{1}}-F^{[\ell],\rho_{2}}_{t})(\varphi)\|_{L^{2,-n}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|Q_{t}\|_{L^{1}}\|(F_{t}^{[\ell],\rho_{1}}-F^{[\ell],\rho_{2}}_{t})(\varphi)\|_{L^{2,-n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-1}\|(F_{t}^{[\ell],\rho_{1}}-F^{[\ell],\rho_{2}}_{t})(\varphi)\|_{L^{2,-n}},

and

\displaystyle\|(F_{t}^{[\ell],\rho_{1}}-F^{[\ell],\rho_{2}}_{t})(\varphi)\|_{L^{2,-n}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{2:\ell}(\rho_{1}-\rho_{2})(\xi_{1:\ell})f^{[\ell]}_{t}(\xi_{1:\ell})\right\|_{L^{2}_{x_{1}}(\langle x\rangle^{-n})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\rho_{1}-\rho_{2}\|_{L^{2,-n}}^{2}\interleave f_{t}^{[\ell]}\interleave^{2}.

The remaining estimate on
F^{[2],(0)}_{t}
follows in the same way as
before, using
Q_{t}
to absorb the increment via
Lemma
A.3
as in (
3.40
)
and (
3.41
) and then following the same steps as above.

∎

Remark 3.14
.

Proposition
3.13
-
c

implies that we can define
Q_{s}F_{s}^{\infty}(\varphi)
,
\mathrm{D}F_{s}^{\infty}(\varphi)Q_{s}
and
H_{s}^{\infty}(\varphi)
as the

L^{\infty}
-limit of
Q_{s}F_{s}^{T}(\varphi)
,
\mathrm{D}F^{T}_{s}(\varphi)Q_{s}

and
H^{T}_{s}(\varphi)
, respectively as
T\rightarrow\infty
.

3.6
Estimates on the potential

The same arguments we used previously for the gradient show the following
estimates on the remainder

\mathcal{H}^{\rho,T}_{t}(\varphi):=\left(\partial_{t}V^{\rho,T}_{t}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{Tr}\dot{G}_{t}\mathrm{D}^{2}V^{\rho,T}_{t}-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{D}V^{\rho,T}_{t}\dot{G_{t}}\mathrm{D}V^{\rho,T}_{t}\right)(\varphi),

(3.46)

at the level of the potential. We should emphasise that in contrast to the
estimates on
F
, they of course only apply in the finite volume, that is for

\rho\prec 1
. The results of this section will be used only later on to recover
the variational description for the unregularised measures in
Section
7
and we invite the reader skip them at first reading.

Proposition 3.15
.

Given a set
A\subset\mdmathbb{R}^{2}
, denote its
Lebesgue measure by
|A|
. For any spatial cut-off
\rho\prec 1
, UV cut-off

T,T_{1},T_{2}\leqslant\infty
, and
\varphi,\tilde{\varphi}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mdmathbb{R}^{2})
, it holds that

a)

(Lipschitz estimates)

|\mathcal{H}^{\rho}_{t}(\varphi)-\mathcal{H}^{\rho}_{t}(\tilde{\varphi})|\lesssim|\operatorname{supp}(\rho)|(\bar{\lambda}_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-1})^{4}\|\varphi-\tilde{\varphi}\|_{L^{\infty}},\quad t\in[0,T].

b)

(Dependence on the regularisation)

There is an
\varepsilon>0
such that

|(\mathcal{H}_{t}^{\rho,T_{1}}-\mathcal{H}^{\rho,T_{2}}_{t})(\varphi)|\lesssim|\operatorname{supp}(\rho)|\langle T_{1}\wedge T_{2}\rangle^{-\varepsilon},\quad t\in[0,T],

and

|\mathcal{H}_{t}^{\rho,T}(\varphi)-\mathcal{H}^{\rho,T}_{t}(\tilde{\varphi})|\lesssim\|\rho\|_{L^{2,-n}}\|\varphi-\tilde{\varphi}\|_{L^{2,n}}.\quad t\in[0,T].

(3.47)

Proof.

By the definition (
3.46
) of
\mathcal{H}^{\rho,T}
above, we see
that
\mathcal{H}^{\rho,T}
has the Fourier series representation

\mathcal{H}_{t}^{\rho,T}(\varphi):=\sum_{\ell=4}^{6}\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{1:\ell}\rho(\xi_{1:\ell})h^{T}_{t}(\xi_{1:\ell})\psi(\xi_{1:\ell}),

(3.48)

where we used the notation
\rho(\xi_{1:\ell})=\prod_{k\leqslant\ell}\rho(x_{k})
and for
\ell\in\{4,5,6\}
, we defined

h^{T}_{t}(\xi_{1:\ell}):=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}[]{c}I_{1}\cup I_{2}=[\ell]\end{array}$}}}C(|I_{1}|,|I_{2}|)f_{t}^{T}(\xi_{I_{1}})\left[\sum_{i\in I_{1}}\sum_{j\in I_{2}}\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\dot{G}_{t}(x_{i}-x_{j})\right]f_{t}^{T}(\xi_{I_{2}}),

(3.49)

for a positive combinatorial constant
C(|I_{1}|,|I_{2}|)
. Moreover, by
Remark
3.8
, we have the estimate

\interleave h^{[\ell],T}_{t}\interleave\lesssim(\bar{\lambda}_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-1})^{\ell}.

(3.50)

a)

This follows directly from the definition of
\mathcal{H}^{\rho}
,
the bounds on the kernels (
3.50
) and simple
rearrangements, using again the boundedness of
|\psi(\xi)|=1
,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{\ell=4}^{6}\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{1:\ell}\rho(\xi_{1:\ell})h^{T}_{t}(\xi_{1:\ell})(\psi(\xi_{1:\ell})-\tilde{\psi}(\xi_{1:\ell}))\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=4}^{6}\int\mathrm{d}x_{1}\rho(x_{1})\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{2:\ell}h^{T}_{t}(\xi_{1:\ell})|\psi(\xi_{1:\ell})-\tilde{\psi}(\xi_{1:\ell})|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle|\operatorname{supp}(\rho)|\sum_{\ell=4}^{6}\sup_{\xi_{1}}\int\mathrm{d}\xi_{2:\ell}|h^{T}_{t}(\xi_{1:\ell})|\|\varphi-\tilde{\varphi}\|_{L^{\infty}},

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|\operatorname{supp}(\rho)|\|\varphi-\tilde{\varphi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\sum_{\ell=4}^{6}\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{\ell}\langle t\rangle^{-\ell}.

b)

This proof is in complete analogy to Proposition

3.13
-
c

and
d
using the same ideas as in Proposition

3.15
-
a
above.

∎

4
Analysis of the FBSDE

With good approximate solutions to the flow under the conditional expectation
and the renormalisation sorted out, we can return to the FBSDE

\begin{cases}X_{t}^{\rho,T,g}=\varphi+W_{t}-\int_{0}^{t}\dot{G}_{s}(F^{\rho,T}_{s}(X_{s}^{\rho,T,g})+R_{s}^{\rho,T,g})\mathrm{d}s,\\
R^{\rho,T,g}_{t}=\mdmathbb{E}_{t}\left[\nabla g(X_{T}^{g})+\int_{t}^{T}H^{\rho,T}_{s}(X_{s}^{\rho,T,g})\mathrm{d}s-\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{D}F^{\rho,T}_{s}(X_{s}^{\rho,T,g})\dot{G}_{s}R_{s}^{\rho,T}\mathrm{d}s\right],\end{cases}

(4.1)

with
F
and
H
as defined in Section
3.4
.

We will often work with
Z^{\rho,T,g}:=X_{t}^{\rho,T,g}-(W_{t}+\varphi)
directly to obtain deterministic bounds on the drift
Z
. To lighten
the notation, we leave the dependence of the solution on
T
and
\rho

implicit whenever possible and fix the perturbation
g
. Unless
explicitly stated otherwise, all estimates are uniform in the parameters

\rho
and
T
.

We will furthermore always implicitly assume that the solution
to (
4.1
) is extended to the positive half line
[0,\infty)

in the standard way, that is

(X_{t}^{T},R_{t}^{T}):=(X_{T\wedge t}^{T},R_{T\wedge t}^{T}),\quad t\in[0,\infty).

4.1
Well-posedness for the FBSDE

As a first step, we show well-posedness for the FBSDE (
4.1
)
with the regularisations in place. We follow a standard Picard-iteration for
the solution map
\Gamma(z)=Z^{z}
defined by

\begin{cases}Z^{z}_{t}=-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\dot{G}_{s}(F_{s}(z_{s}+W_{s})+R^{z}_{s}),\\
R^{z}_{t}=\mdmathbb{E}_{t}\left[\nabla g(z_{s}+W_{T})+\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}sH_{s}(z_{s}+W_{s})-\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{D}F_{s}(z_{s}+W_{s})\dot{G}_{s}R^{z}_{s}\right].\end{cases}

(4.2)

Standard well-posedness for decoupled Lipschitz FBSDEs ensures the existence
of a unique solution
(Z^{z},R^{z})\in\mdmathbb{H}^{\infty}_{T}(L^{\infty})\times\mdmathbb{H}^{\infty}_{T}(L^{\infty})
to (
4.2
) for any
z\in\mdmathbb{H}^{\infty}_{T}(L^{\infty})
. The only term in (
4.2
)
that cannot be estimated in a linear fashion immediately from Proposition

3.13
is the term
\mathrm{D}F_{s}(z_{s}+W_{s})\dot{G}_{s}R^{z}_{s}

in the backward equation. The next Lemma ensures that also this term stays
bounded and does not cause any issues.

Lemma 4.1
.

For all in
z\in\mdmathbb{H}^{\infty}_{T}(L^{\infty})
and

\lambda\in\mdmathbb{R}
(not necessarily small), there are constants
C_{\bar{\lambda}},C_{g,\bar{\lambda}}
non-decreasing in
\bar{\lambda}

\sup_{t}\|R^{z}_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim C_{\bar{\lambda}}(|g|_{1,\infty}+\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{4}\langle t\rangle^{-3})<\infty,\quad\text{and}\quad\sup_{t}\|Z^{z}_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant C_{g,\bar{\lambda}}

Proof.

From the definition of
R^{z}
, the regularity of
W_{t}\in L^{2,-n}
for any

t<\infty
and the bounds on the flow from Proposition

3.13
-
a
,

\displaystyle\|R_{t}^{z}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\bar{\lambda}|g|_{1,\infty}+\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\|H_{s}(z_{s}+W_{s})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\bar{\lambda}\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\|\mathrm{D}F_{s}(z_{s})\dot{G}_{s}R^{z}_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}},

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}|g|_{1,\infty}+\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\bar{\lambda}_{s}^{4}\langle s\rangle^{-4}+\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\bar{\lambda}_{s}\langle s\rangle^{-2}\|R^{z}_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

By a backward version of Gronwall’s inequality, this implies with
4\delta>1
(see Lemma
3.1
),

\|R_{t}^{z}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\left(\bar{\lambda}|g|_{1,\infty}+\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\bar{\lambda}_{s}^{4}\langle s\rangle^{-4}\right)\mathrm{e}^{\bar{\lambda}\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\langle s\rangle^{-1-\delta}}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}|g|_{1,\infty}+\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{4}\langle t\rangle^{-3}.

(4.3)

Thus, using the bound just derived for
R^{z}
in the equation for
Z^{z}
,

\|Z^{z}_{t}\|\leqslant\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\|\dot{G}_{s}(F_{s}(z_{s}+W_{s})+R^{z}_{s})\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\langle s\rangle^{-2}(\bar{\lambda}_{s}+\|R_{s}^{z}\|_{L^{\infty}})\lesssim\bar{\lambda}C_{g}.

∎

With this issue resolved, we are in a position to show
that (
4.2
) defines a contraction on
H^{\infty}_{T}(L^{\infty})
.

Proposition 4.2
.

For
\bar{\lambda}
sufficiently small, the map
\Gamma:\mdmathbb{H}^{\infty}_{T}(L^{\infty})\rightarrow\mdmathbb{H}^{\infty}_{T}(L^{\infty});z\mapsto Z^{z}
is a contraction.

Since
Z
uniquely determines the solution
(X,R)
to (
4.1
)
via

Z\mapsto(\varphi+Z_{t}+W_{t},R^{Z}_{t})_{t\geqslant 0},

this immediately implies the existence of a unique solution to
(
4.1
) when combined with the regularity of the stopped
Brownian motion
(W_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}\in\mdmathbb{H}^{2}_{T}(L^{2,-n})
.

Corollary 4.3
.

For any
\rho\preceq 1,T<\infty
,
\bar{\lambda}
sufficiently small and
p\in[1,\infty)
the FBSDE (
4.1
) has a unique solution

(X_{t},R_{t})_{t\geqslant 0}=(\varphi+Z_{t}+W_{t},R_{t})_{t\geqslant 0}\in\mdmathbb{H}^{p}_{T}(L^{p,-n})\times\mdmathbb{H}^{\infty}_{T}(L^{\infty}).

Proof of Proposition
4.2
.

Let
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mdmathbb{H}^{\infty}_{T}(L^{\infty})
and consider the FBSDE
for the difference
(\delta Z,\delta R)=(Z^{z_{1}},R^{z_{1}})-(Z^{z_{2}},R^{z_{2}})
given by

\begin{cases}\delta Z_{t}=-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\dot{G}_{s}(F_{s}(X^{z_{1}}_{s})-F_{s}(X^{z_{2}}_{s})+\delta R_{s})\\
\delta R_{t}=\mdmathbb{E}_{t}\left[\delta_{z}\nabla g+\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\delta_{z}H_{s}-\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s(\mathrm{D}F_{s}(X^{z_{1}}_{s})\dot{G_{s}}R^{z_{1}}_{s}-\mathrm{D}F_{s}(X^{z_{2}}_{s})\dot{G_{s}}R^{z_{2}}_{s})\right],\end{cases}

(4.4)

where we use the shorthand
X_{s}^{z}=\varphi+z_{s}+W_{s}
so that
\delta X_{s}=\delta z_{s}
and

\delta_{z}\nabla g:=\nabla g(X^{z_{1}}_{s})-\nabla g(X^{z_{2}}_{s}),\quad\delta_{z}H_{s}:=H_{s}(X^{z_{1}}_{s})-H_{s}(X^{z_{2}}_{s}).

To deal with the bilinear term in the backward equation, we combine the
estimates from Proposition
3.13
-
a

with the boundedness of
R^{z}
provided by Lemma
4.1
to conclude

\displaystyle\|(\mathrm{D}F_{s}(X^{z_{1}}_{s})-\mathrm{D}F_{s}(X^{z_{2}}_{s}))\dot{G_{s}}R^{z_{2}}_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|R^{z_{2}}_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|[\mathrm{D}F_{s}(X^{z_{1}}_{s})-\mathrm{D}F_{s}(X^{z_{2}}_{s})]\dot{G}_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{s}\langle s\rangle^{-2}\|\delta z_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

The remaining terms in the backward equation can all be estimated directly
using Proposition
3.13
-
a
and the
Lipschitz continuity of
\nabla g
,

\displaystyle\|\delta R_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\bar{\lambda}_{s}^{4}\langle s\rangle^{-4}\|\delta z_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\bar{\lambda}_{s}\langle s\rangle^{-2}\|\delta R\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\bar{\lambda}_{s}\langle s\rangle^{-2}\|\delta z_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}},

which implies by Gronwall’s inequality,

\sup_{t}\|\delta R_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant C\bar{\lambda}\sup_{t}\|\delta z_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Using this estimate on
R
in the equation for the forward component with
the Lipschitz estimates from Proposition

3.13
-
a
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{t}\|\delta Z_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\int_{0}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\|\dot{G}_{s}(F_{s}(X^{z_{1}}_{s})-F_{s}(X^{z_{2}}_{s})+\delta R_{s})\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\bar{\lambda}\int_{0}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\langle s\rangle^{-2}\bar{\lambda}_{s}\|\delta z_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\langle s\rangle^{-2}\|\delta R\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\bar{\lambda}\sup_{s}\|\delta z_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\sup_{s}\|\delta R_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\bar{\lambda}\sup_{s}\|\delta z_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}},

which yields the required contraction for
\bar{\lambda}
small enough.
∎

Remark 4.4
.

We crucially rely on the uniform, field independent
estimates on the approximate force obtained in
Proposition
3.13
which hold only up to
6\pi
.
Indeed, assuming only a weaker estimate like (
3.38
), it is
unclear how to obtain suitable replacements for the a priori estimates of
Lemma
4.1
and rule out explosion in finite time. Even linear
growth in
\mathrm{D}F_{t}(\varphi)
would require an additional argument as any
trivial estimate for the backward equation results in an exponential
dependence on
\|Z\|_{L^{\infty}}
in the equation for the remainder
through (
4.3
).

4.2
Stability properties

In this section, we show that the FBSDE (
4.1
) is stable in both regularisations
\rho
and
T
,
provided that the coupling constant
\bar{\lambda}
is chosen sufficiently small. We
summarise these properties below.

Proposition 4.5
.

For
\bar{\lambda}
sufficiently small and
n>2
(so
that
x\mapsto\langle x\rangle^{-n}\in L^{1}(\mdmathbb{R}^{2})
), the
following stability estimates hold.

a)

Dependence on the spatial cut-off:

Let
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}\leqslant 1
and denote
the associated solutions to (
4.1
) by
(Z^{\rho_{1}},R^{\rho_{1}})
and
(Z^{\rho_{2}},R^{\rho_{2}})
respectively. Then, the
difference between the solution
(\delta_{\rho}Z,\delta_{\rho}R):=(Z^{\rho_{1}},R^{\rho_{1}})-(Z^{\rho_{2}},R^{\rho_{2}})
satisfies

\sup_{t}\|\delta_{\rho}Z_{t}\|_{L^{2,-n}}+\sup_{t}\|\delta_{\rho}R_{t}\|_{L^{2,-n}}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}\|\rho_{1}-\rho_{2}\|_{L^{2,-n}}.

b)

Dependence on the UV cut-off:

Let
T_{1},T_{2}<\infty
and denote the associated solutions
to (
4.1
) by
(Z^{T_{1}},R^{T_{1}})
and
(Z^{T_{2}},R^{T_{2}})

respectively. Then, the difference between the solution
(\delta_{T}Z,\delta_{T}R):=(Z^{T_{1}},R^{T_{1}})-(Z^{T_{2}},R^{T_{2}})
satisfies for
some
\varepsilon>0
,

\sup_{t}\|\delta_{T}Z_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\sup_{t}\|\delta_{T}R_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\langle T\rangle^{-\varepsilon}.

c)

Dependence on local
perturbations:
Let
(Z^{\rho,T,g},R^{\rho,T,g})
be the unique solution to (
4.1
). It holds
that

\sup_{t}\|Z^{\rho,T,g}_{t}-Z_{t}^{\rho,T,0}\|_{L^{2,n}}+\sup_{t}\|R_{t}^{\rho,T,g}-R_{t}^{\rho,T,0}\|_{L^{2,n}}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}|g|_{1,2,n}.

Proof.

a)

We follow essentially the same argument as before for the proof of
Proposition
4.2
, writing the FBSDE for the difference
as

\begin{cases}\delta_{\rho}Z_{t}=-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\dot{G}_{s}(F_{s}^{\rho_{1}}(X^{\rho_{1}}_{s})-F_{s}^{\rho_{2}}(X_{s}^{\rho_{2}})+\delta_{\rho}R_{s})\\
\delta_{\rho}R_{t}=\mdmathbb{E}_{t}\left[\delta_{\rho}\nabla g+\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\left(\delta_{\rho}H_{s}+\mathrm{D}F_{s}^{\rho_{1}}\left(X_{s}^{\rho_{1}}\right)\dot{G}_{s}R_{s}^{\rho_{1}}-\mathrm{D}F_{s}^{\rho_{2}}(X_{s}^{\rho_{2}})\dot{G}_{s}R_{s}^{\rho_{2}}\right)\right]\end{cases}

(4.5)

where
\delta_{\rho}\nabla g=\nabla g(X^{\rho_{1}}_{T})-\nabla g(X^{\rho_{1}}_{T})
and
\delta_{\rho}H_{s}=H_{s}^{\rho_{1}}(X^{\rho_{1}}_{s})-H_{s}^{\rho_{2}}(X^{\rho_{2}}_{s})
. Using the estimates from Proposition

3.13
-
d
in the FBSDE for
the difference (
4.5
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{t}\|\delta_{\rho}Z_{t}\|_{L^{2,-n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\bar{\lambda}_{s}\langle s\rangle^{-2}(\|\delta_{\rho}Z_{s}\|_{L^{2,-n}}+\|\rho_{1}-\rho_{2}\|_{L^{2,-n}}+\|\delta_{\rho}R_{s}\|_{L^{2,-n}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}(\sup_{s}\|\delta_{\rho}Z_{s}\|_{L^{2,-n}}+\|\rho_{1}-\rho_{2}\|_{L^{2,-n}})+\sup_{s}\|\delta_{\rho}R_{s}\|_{L^{2,-n}},

and

\displaystyle\sup_{t}\|\delta_{\rho}R_{t}\|_{L^{2,-n}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\bar{\lambda}_{s}\langle s\rangle^{-2}(\|\delta_{\rho}Z_{s}\|_{L^{2,-n}}+\|\rho_{1}-\rho_{2}\|_{L^{2,-n}})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\bar{\lambda}_{s}\langle s\rangle^{-2}[\|\rho_{1}-\rho_{2}\|_{L^{2,-n}}+\|\delta_{\rho}Z_{s}\|_{L^{2,-n}}+\|\delta_{\rho}R_{s}\|_{L^{2,-n}}]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}(\sup_{s}\|\delta_{\rho}Z_{s}\|_{L^{2,-n}}+\sup_{s}\|\delta_{\rho}R_{s}\|_{L^{2,-n}}+\|\rho_{1}-\rho_{2}\|_{L^{2,-n}}),

which yields the claim after choosing
\bar{\lambda}
sufficiently small and
rearranging.

b)

For concreteness, let
T_{2}<T_{1}
. The difference between the two
solutions solves the FBSDE,

\begin{cases}\delta_{T}Z_{t}=-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\dot{G}_{s}(F_{s}^{T_{1}}(X^{T_{1}}_{s})-F_{s}^{T_{2}}(X^{T_{2}}_{s})+\delta_{T}R_{s})\\
\delta_{T}R_{t}=\mdmathbb{E}_{t}\left[\delta_{T}\nabla g+\mdmathbb{E}_{t}\int_{t}^{T}\delta_{T}H_{s}\mathrm{d}s\right]\\
\phantom{\delta_{T}R_{t}=}+\int_{t}^{T}\left(-(\mathrm{D}F_{s}^{T_{1}}\left(X_{s}^{T_{1}}\right)\dot{G}_{s}R_{s}^{T_{1}}-\mathrm{D}F_{s}^{T_{2}}(X_{s}^{T_{2}})\dot{G}_{s}R_{s}^{T_{2}})\right)\mathrm{d}s,\end{cases}

where
\delta_{T}\nabla g=\nabla g(X^{T_{1}}_{T_{1}})-\nabla g(X^{T_{2}}_{T_{2}})

and
\delta_{T}H_{s}=H_{s}^{T_{1}}(X^{T_{1}}_{s})-H_{s}^{T_{2}}(X^{T_{2}}_{s})
. The
only difference to the estimates before is the additional tail in the
backward equation. Other than that we proceed as before. For the forward
equation, splitting up the differences as in the proof of part

a
, we have from Proposition

3.13
-
a

and
3.13
-
c
for
\varepsilon
sufficiently small,

\displaystyle\|\delta_{T}Z_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}\sup_{s}\|\delta_{T}Z_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\delta_{T}R_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\langle T_{2}\rangle^{-\varepsilon}.

For the backward equation, we split the terms as

\displaystyle\delta_{T}R_{t}=

\displaystyle\nabla g(X^{T_{1}}_{T_{1}})-\nabla g(X^{T_{2}}_{T_{2}})

\displaystyle+\mdmathbb{E}_{t}\int_{t}^{T_{2}}\mathrm{d}s(H_{s}^{T_{1}}(X^{T_{1}}_{s})-H_{s}^{T_{2}}(X^{T_{2}}_{s}))

\displaystyle-\mdmathbb{E}_{t}\int_{t}^{T_{2}}\mathrm{d}s(\mathrm{D}F_{s}^{T_{1}}(X_{s}^{T_{1}})\dot{G}_{s}R_{s}^{T_{1}}-\mathrm{D}F_{s}^{T_{2}}(X_{s}^{T_{2}})\dot{G}_{s}R_{s}^{T_{2}})

\displaystyle+\mdmathbb{E}_{t}\int_{T_{2}}^{T_{1}}\mathrm{d}sH_{s}^{T_{1}}(X^{T_{1}}_{s})-\mdmathbb{E}_{t}\int_{T_{2}}^{T_{1}}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{D}F_{s}^{T_{1}}(X_{s}^{T_{1}}\dot{G}_{s}R_{s}^{T_{1}}),

we proceed similarly on
[0,T_{2}]
to obtain from Proposition

3.13
-
c

and
3.13
-
a
,

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{T_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\|H_{s}^{T_{1}}(X^{T_{1}}_{s})-H_{s}^{T_{2}}(X^{T_{2}}_{s})\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}\sup_{s}\|\delta_{T}Z_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\bar{\lambda}\langle T_{2}\rangle^{-\varepsilon},

and

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{T_{2}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}s\|\mathrm{D}F_{s}^{T_{1}}(X_{s}^{T_{1}})\dot{G}_{s}R_{s}^{T_{1}}-\mathrm{D}F_{s}^{T_{2}}(X_{s}^{T_{2}})\dot{G}_{s}R_{s}^{T_{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}(\sup_{s}\|\delta_{T}Z_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\sup_{s}\|\delta_{T}R_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\langle T_{2}\rangle^{-\varepsilon}).

The integrals on
[T_{2},T_{1}]
can be estimated using just the boundedness
of the coefficients provided by Proposition

3.13
-
a
,

\displaystyle\int_{T_{2}}^{T_{1}}\mathrm{d}s\|H_{s}^{T_{1}}(X^{T_{1}}_{s})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{T_{2}}^{T_{1}}\mathrm{d}s\|\mathrm{D}F_{s}^{T_{1}}(X_{s}^{T_{1}})\dot{G}_{s}R_{s}^{T_{1}}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}\int_{T_{2}}^{T_{1}}\mathrm{d}s\langle s\rangle^{-4\delta}+\langle s\rangle^{-1-\delta}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}\langle T_{2}\rangle^{-\varepsilon}.

Finally, by the regularity assumed on
g
,

\|\nabla g(X^{T_{1}}_{T_{1}})-\nabla g(X^{T_{2}}_{T_{2}})\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}|g|_{2,\infty}\|X^{T_{1}}_{T_{2}}-X^{T_{2}}_{T_{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}\sup_{s}\|\delta X_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Combining all of the above yields the claim for
\bar{\lambda}
small enough
after rearranging.

c)

This proof is straightforward and does not require any new arguments
compared to e.g. the proof of part
a
. Indeed, now
the FBSDE for the difference is

\begin{cases}\delta_{g}Z_{t}=-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\dot{G}_{s}(F_{s}(X^{g}_{s})-F_{s}(X^{0}_{s})+\delta_{g}R_{s})\\
\delta_{g}R_{t}=\nabla g(X^{g}_{T})+\mdmathbb{E}_{t}\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\delta_{g}H_{s}-\mdmathbb{E}_{t}\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\left(\mathrm{D}F_{s}(X^{g}_{s})\dot{G}_{s}R^{g}_{s}-\mathrm{D}F_{s}(X_{s}^{0})\dot{G}_{s}R_{s}^{0}\right),\end{cases}

where again
\delta_{g}H_{s}=H_{s}(X^{g}_{s})-H_{s}(X^{0}_{s})
. The same steps as
in part
a
and
b
imply the
claim with the Lipschitz estimates from Proposition

3.13
-
b
.

∎

4.3
Recovering the EQFT

Throughout this section, we assume that
\bar{\lambda}
is chosen small enough for
Corollary
4.3
and Proposition
4.5
to
apply. Then, for any
\rho\prec 1
and
T<\infty
, we denote by
(X^{\rho,T},R^{\rho,T})
the unique solution to (
4.1
). We show the
following refined version of Theorem
1.1
.

Theorem 4.6
.

As the cut-offs are removed, the family
\{(Z^{\rho,T},R^{\rho,T})\}_{\rho\preceq 1,T<\infty}
converges in

\mdmathbb{H}^{2}(L^{2,-n})\times\mdmathbb{H}^{2}(L^{2,-n})
to a unique
limit
(Z,R)\in\mdmathbb{H}^{\infty}(L^{\infty})\times\mdmathbb{H}^{\infty}(L^{\infty})
, that is

\lim_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}[]{c}\rho\rightarrow 1\\
T\rightarrow\infty\end{array}$}}}\sup_{t}\{\|Z^{\rho,T}_{t}-Z_{t}\|_{L^{2,-n}}+\|R^{\rho,T}_{t}-R_{t}\|_{L^{2,-n}}\}=0.

The limiting process is the unique solution to (
4.1
) with
T=\infty,\rho=1
.
Moreover, for any
\varepsilon>0
,
p\in[0,\infty)
and
\varphi\in B^{0-\varepsilon,-n}_{p,p}
, there is a version of the drift process

Z
with terminal value
Z_{\infty}\in L^{\infty}\left(\mathrm{d}\mdmathbb{P};\;B_{p,p}^{2-\beta^{2}/4\pi-\varepsilon,-n}\right)
, so that

X_{\infty}=Z_{\infty}+(\varphi+W_{\infty})\in L^{\infty}\left(\mathrm{d}\mdmathbb{P};\;B_{p,p}^{2-\beta^{2}/4\pi-\varepsilon,-n}\right)+L^{p}(\mathrm{d}\mdmathbb{P};B_{p,p}^{0-\varepsilon,-n}).

(4.6)

In particular, for any
\varepsilon>0
, the family

(\nu_{\operatorname{SG}}^{\rho,T})_{\rho,T}
has a unique weak limit in
H^{-\varepsilon,-n}
as
\rho\rightarrow 1
and
T\rightarrow\infty
which
we denote by
\nu_{\operatorname{SG}}
. It is given as a random shift of the
Gaussian free field,

\operatorname{Law}(Z_{T}^{\rho,T}+W_{T})=\nu^{\rho,T}_{\operatorname{SG}}\longrightarrow\nu_{\operatorname{SG}}=\operatorname{Law}(Z_{\infty}+W_{\infty}).

For
\beta^{2}<4\pi
, we obtain
Z_{\infty}\in H^{1+,-n}
and in the
finite volume, that is for
\rho\prec 1
, the same argument in the unweighted
spaces implies
Z^{\rho}_{\infty}\in H^{1}
. In this case, the Wick ordered cosine
\llbracket\cos(\beta W_{t})\rrbracket

converges in
H^{\alpha}
for any
\alpha<-\beta^{2}/4\pi\in(-1,0)
, and we can define
all products on the right-hand side of

\llbracket\cos(\beta(Z_{\infty}+W_{\infty}))\rrbracket:=\cos(\beta Z_{\infty})\llbracket\cos(\beta W_{\infty})\rrbracket-\sin(\beta Z_{\infty})\llbracket\sin(\beta W_{\infty})\rrbracket,

(4.7)

so that the partition function
\Xi^{\rho}=\mdmathbb{E}[\exp(-\bar{\lambda}V^{\rho,\infty}(Z_{\infty}+W_{\infty}))]
stays bounded. Consequently, we
recover that the law of the shift
\nu_{\operatorname{SG}}^{\rho}=\operatorname{Law}(Z_{\infty}^{\rho}+W_{\infty})
is absolutely continuous with respect to the
Gaussian free field
\mu=\operatorname{Law}(W_{\infty})
. For
\beta^{2}\geqslant 4\pi
, this is no longer the case (see Theorem
1.4
and
Theorem
6.1
) and indeed Theorem
4.6

only ensures the regularity
Z^{\rho}_{\infty}\in H^{2-\beta^{2}/4\pi-}(\mdmathbb{R}^{2})=H^{1/2+}(\mdmathbb{R}^{2})
, which we conjecture to be
optimal for
\beta^{2}<6\pi
. This regularity no longer allows to define the products on the right-hand side of (
4.7
).

Proof of Theorem
4.6
.

The fact that the limit exists follows from Proposition

4.5
-
a

and
b
. Note that all constants are uniform in
\rho\leqslant 1
and
T\leqslant\infty
, so the order in which we take the
limits is irrelevant. Denote the limiting processes of
(Z^{\rho,T},R^{\rho,T})_{\rho,T}
by
(Z,R)
and let
X:=\varphi+Z+W
.
Then, the aforementioned convergence results transfer the bound from
Lemma
4.1
to the limit so that,

\|Z_{\infty}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|R_{\infty}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant\sup_{t}(\|Z_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|R_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}})\lesssim 1.

The convergence of
W_{t}
to the Gaussian free field in
H^{0-,-n}
is the
content of Lemma
2.1
and

\operatorname{Law}(Z_{T}^{\rho,T}+W_{T})=\nu_{\operatorname{SG}}^{\rho,T},

was already shown in Theorem
2.2
-
b
. By
Gaussian hypercontractivity and the Besov embeddings,
for any
p\in[0,\infty]
there is a version
of the free field such that
W_{\infty}\in B_{p,p}^{0-,-n}
holds and it
remains to show only that the drift
Z_{t}
has a terminal value
Z_{\infty}

with the required regularity. Thanks to Lemma
A.5
, for
any
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\varepsilon>0
,

\|Z_{\infty}\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha}}=\left\|\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\dot{G}_{s}(F_{s}(X_{s})+R_{s})\right\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha,-n}}\lesssim\sup_{s}\langle s\rangle^{\alpha/2+\varepsilon}\|Q_{s}(F_{s}(X_{s})+R_{s})\|_{L^{\infty}}.

(4.8)

Using Proposition
3.13
and Lemma
4.1
we
know

\|Q_{s}(F_{s}+R_{s})\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\langle s\rangle^{-1}\bar{\lambda}_{s}+\langle s\rangle^{-1}\lesssim s^{-\delta}=\langle s\rangle^{\beta^{2}/8\pi-1}.

Therefore, we can choose
\varepsilon>0
small enough for
(
4.8
) to be finite provided

\alpha<2-\beta^{2}/4\pi=2\delta.

∎

As a direct consequence of Theorem
4.6
, we also get
exponential moments for the limiting measure.

Corollary 4.7
.

For any
\varepsilon>0
, there is a constant

\gamma>0
such that

\int_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mdmathbb{R}^{2})}\mathrm{e}^{\gamma\|\varphi\|_{H^{-\varepsilon,-n}}^{2}}\nu_{\operatorname{SG}}(\mathrm{d}\varphi)<\infty.

Proof.

By Fernique’s theorem (see e.g.
[
15
, Theorem
2.8.5.]
) the Gaussian free field
W_{\infty}

has squared exponential moments in
H^{-\varepsilon,-n}
for some
\gamma>0
. Combined with the bounds on
Z_{\infty}
from Theorem

4.6
,

\int_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mdmathbb{R}^{2})}\mathrm{e}^{\gamma\|\varphi\|_{H^{-\varepsilon,-n}}^{2}}\nu_{\operatorname{SG}}(\mathrm{d}\varphi)\lesssim\mdmathbb{E}[\exp(\gamma\|W_{\infty}\|_{H^{-\varepsilon,-n}}^{2}+\gamma\|Z_{\infty}\|_{L^{2,-n}}^{2})]<\infty.

∎

For future reference, let us also note the following regularity property of
the solution.

Lemma 4.8
.

For any
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
\varepsilon>0
and
p\in[1,\infty]
, the solution to the FBSDE (
4.1
) satisfies

\mdmathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t}\,\langle t\rangle^{-\alpha/2}\|W_{t}\|_{B^{\alpha-\varepsilon,-n}_{p,p}}\right]\lesssim 1,\quad\sup_{t}\,\langle t\rangle^{-\alpha/2+\delta}\|Z_{t}\|_{B^{\alpha-\varepsilon,-n}_{p,p}}\lesssim 1.

In particular,
\sup_{t}\langle t\rangle^{-\alpha/2}\|X_{t}\|_{B^{\alpha-\varepsilon,-n}_{p,p}}<\infty
almost surely.

Proof.

For the estimate on the field
(W_{t})_{t\geqslant 0}
, we refer to
Appendix
A.4
. The estimate on
Z_{t}
follows in the
same way as (
4.8
). Combining the estimates for
Z
and
W

yields the estimate for
(X_{t})_{t\geqslant 0}
.
∎

4.4
Uniqueness for the finite volume measure

The convergence to a
unique
measure in Theorem
4.6
requires the
coupling constant
\bar{\lambda}
to be sufficiently small to close the argument for
the coupled forward backward system. In this section, we show that the limiting measure is still unique as long as the volume remains finite. We do
not expect this to be true for large values of the coupling constant in the
infinite volume. Let us fix
g=0
and a volume cut-off
\rho\prec 1
left
implicit throughout this section.

Proposition 4.9
.

Let
\bar{\lambda}\in\mdmathbb{R},\hskip 1.69998ptT\in[0,\infty],\rho\prec 1
and let
(Z,R)=(Z^{\rho},R^{\rho})
be any accumulation point of
(Z^{\rho,T},R^{\rho,T})_{T\in[0,\infty)}
. Then,
\operatorname{Law(Z_{\infty}+W_{\infty})}
is unique.

Proof.

Let O be bounded and continuous
H^{-\varepsilon,-n}(\mdmathbb{R}^{2})\rightarrow\mdmathbb{R}
for some
\varepsilon>0
. The claim follows once we show that

X_{T}^{\rho,T}=Z_{T}^{\rho,T}+W_{T}
satisfies

\lim_{T\rightarrow\infty}\mdmathbb{E}[\mathcal{O}(X^{T}_{T})]=\frac{\mdmathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{O}(\bar{X}_{\infty})\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{H}_{s}(\bar{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}s}\right]}{\mdmathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{H}_{s}(\bar{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}s}\right]},

(4.9)

where
\bar{X}
is the unique strong solution to

\bar{X}_{t}=-\int_{0}^{t}\dot{G}_{s}F_{s}(\bar{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}s+W_{t}.

(4.10)

First, let us note that the estimates on
\dot{G}F
(bounded and Lipschitz) from Proposition
3.13
guarantee existence of a unique strong solution to (
4.10
). The same is true for
\bar{X}^{T}
the solution to

\bar{X}^{T}_{t}=-\int_{0}^{t}\dot{G}_{s}F_{s}^{T}(\bar{X}^{T}_{s})\mathrm{d}s+W_{t}\qquad t\in[0,T].

(4.11)

Moreover, the estimates on
F^{T},F
in Proposition
3.13
and the arguments used in the proof of Theorem
4.6
show that
\bar{X}^{T}
converges to
\bar{X}
and that
\bar{X}
has a terminal value
\bar{X}_{\infty}
. More precisely, with
\bar{Z}=\bar{X}-W
,

\lim_{T\to\infty}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\bar{X}_{s}^{T}-\bar{X}_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mdmathbb{P};L^{\infty})}=0.

(4.12)

By definition of
X
, we know that

\mdmathbb{E}[\mathcal{O}(X_{T}^{T})]=\frac{\mdmathbb{E}[\mathcal{O}(W_{T})\mathrm{e}^{-V_{T}^{T}(W_{T})}]}{\mdmathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{-V_{T}^{T}(W_{T})}]}=\frac{\mdmathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{O}(W_{T})\mathrm{e}^{-V_{0}^{T}(W_{0})-\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{s}^{T}(W_{s})\mathrm{d}s}\mathcal{E}_{0,T}\right]}{\mdmathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{-V_{0}^{T}(W_{0})-\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{s}^{T}(W_{s})\mathrm{d}s}\mathcal{E}_{0,T}\right]},

where

\mathcal{E}_{t,T}=\exp\left\{-\int_{t\wedge T}^{T}F_{s}^{T}(W_{s})\mathrm{d}W_{s}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{t\wedge T}^{T}F_{s}^{T}(W_{s})\dot{G}_{s}F_{s}^{T}(W_{s})\mathrm{d}s\right\}.

The estimates on
F
from Proposition
3.13
show that
(\mathcal{E}_{t,T})_{t\geq 0}
is a
martingale. By Girsanov’s theorem,

W_{t}^{F}:=W_{t}+\int_{0}^{t}\dot{G}_{s}F_{s}^{T}(W_{s})\mathrm{d}s,

is a Brownian motion under
\mathrm{d}\mdmathbb{P}^{F^{T}}=\mathcal{E}_{0,T}\mathrm{d}\mdmathbb{P}
. Therefore, using that
V_{0}^{T}(W_{0})=V_{0}^{T}(0)
is
deterministic,

\mdmathbb{E}[\mathcal{O}(X_{T}^{T})]=\frac{\mdmathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{O}(\bar{X}_{T}^{T})\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{s}^{T}(\bar{X}_{s}^{T})\mathrm{d}s}\right]}{\mdmathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{H}_{s}^{T}(\bar{X}^{T}_{s})\mathrm{d}s}\right]},

where
\bar{X}^{T}
is the unique strong solution to
(
4.11
).
Combining the convergence of
\bar{X}^{T}_{T}
from (
4.12
) with the convergence of
\mathcal{H}_{s}^{T}
to
\mathcal{H}_{s}
from Proposition
3.15
, we conclude (
4.9
) and thus the claim.
∎

Remark 4.10
.

Let again
\bar{X}
be the unique strong solution to (
4.10
) and denote the set of all
\bar{X}
adapted processes by
\mathcal{A}(\bar{X})
. Consider the
\mdmathbb{R}
-valued BSDE

\mathcal{R}_{t}=\int_{t}^{\infty}\mathcal{H}_{s}(\bar{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}s-\frac{1}{2}\int_{t}^{\infty}\|\dot{G}_{s}^{1/2}\bar{R}_{s}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\mathrm{d}s-\int_{t}^{\infty}\bar{R}_{s}\mathrm{d}W_{s}.

(4.13)

The estimates on
\mathcal{H}
and
\bar{X}
, imply that (
4.13
)
has a unique bounded solution
(\mathcal{R},\bar{R})
which is also adapted to
\bar{X}
. Moreover, standard
comparison arguments for
\mdmathbb{R}
-valued BSDEs imply that the control

\bar{R}
is the unique optimiser for the following relaxed version of the finite volume control problem discussed in section
7
,

\mathcal{V}^{w}=\inf_{R\in\mathcal{A}(\bar{X})}\mdmathbb{E}^{\mdmathbb{P}^{R}}\left[V_{0}(\bar{X}_{0})+\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{H}_{s}(\bar{X}_{s})\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\dot{G}_{s}^{1/2}R_{s}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right],

where

\mathrm{d}\mdmathbb{P}^{R}=\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{\infty}R_{s}\mathrm{d}W_{s}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\dot{G}_{s}^{1/2}R_{s}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right\}\mathrm{d}\mdmathbb{P},

and
\operatorname{Law}^{\mdmathbb{P}^{\bar{R}}}(\bar{X}_{\infty})=\nu_{\operatorname{SG}}
.

5
Decay of correlations

Using the scale-by-scale coupling via (
4.1
), a coupling method
allows us to transfer the decay of correlations from the massive free field to
the sine-Gordon measure and establish Theorem
1.3
.
We follow mostly
[
27
]
but similar
arguments can be found e.g.
in
[
39
]
and the idea appears to
originate in
[
24
]
.

For simplicity and to not distract from the main ideas, let
\mathcal{O}_{1},\mathcal{O}_{2}:H^{-\varepsilon,-n}\rightarrow\mdmathbb{R}
be two
Lipschitz and bounded observables. Given a smooth bump function
\chi

supported on
B_{1}(0)
we want to show that for some
c>0
, with
X=X_{\infty}

the solution to (
4.1
)

\operatorname{Cov}(\mathcal{O}_{1}(\chi\cdot X(\cdot+x_{1})),\mathcal{O}_{2}(\chi\cdot X(\cdot+x_{2})))\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-c|x_{1}-x_{2}|}.

Let us agree on some notation to use throughout this proof. We denote by

\mathfrak{l}:=|x_{1}-x_{2}|
the distance between the two points of
interest. For
i=1,2
, let
D_{i}(r)
be the open ball of radius
r>0

centred at
x_{i}
, where we drop the argument in case
r=\mathfrak{l}/2
.
Given a smooth bump function
\vartheta^{(i)}
supported on
D_{i}(\mathfrak{l}/4)

such that
\vartheta(x)\equiv 1
on
D_{i}(\mathfrak{l}/8)
, we define the
exponential weights

q^{(i)}(x)=\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m|x-x_{i}|}\quad\text{and}\quad\bar{q}^{(i)}(x)=\vartheta^{(i)}(x)q^{(i)}(x),

In order for the heat kernels
Q
and
G
to work nicely with the weights

q^{(i)}
, we always assume that
\gamma\in(0,1)
. To set up the coupling
argument, let
W^{(0)}:=W
and
D_{0}:=\mathbb{R}^{2}
and define
the identically distributed Brownian motions
W^{(1)},W^{(2)}
with
covariance

\mathbb{E}[W_{t}^{(i)}(x)W_{t}^{(j)}(y)]=\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int\mathrm{d}zQ_{s}(z-x)\mathbbm{1}_{D_{i}\cap D_{j}}(z)Q_{s}(z-y),

so that
W^{(1)}
and
W^{(2)}
are independent and
W^{(0)}\approx W^{(i)}

near
x_{i}
. Denote by
X^{(i)}
the solution to the FBSDE
(
4.1
) with
T=\infty,\rho=1
and
g=0
driven by

W^{(i)}
. Then, the solutions
X^{(1)}
and
X^{(2)}
have the same law as
X

and inherit the
independence from their driving noise. Inserting the
X^{(i)}
for
X
, we
find that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cov}(\mathcal{O}_{1}(\chi\cdot X(\cdot+x_{1})),\mathcal{O}_{2}(\chi\cdot X(\cdot+x_{2})))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{O}_{1}(\chi\cdot X(\cdot+x_{1}))\mathcal{O}_{2}(\chi\cdot X(\cdot+x_{2}))]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{O}_{1}(\chi\cdot X^{(1)}(\cdot+x_{1}))]\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{O}_{2}(\chi\cdot X^{(2)}(\cdot+x_{2}))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{O}_{2}(\chi\cdot X^{(2)}(\cdot+x_{2}))\;(\mathcal{O}_{1}(\chi\cdot X(\cdot+x_{1}))-\mathcal{O}_{1}(\chi\cdot X^{(1)}(\cdot+x_{1})))]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{O}_{1}(\chi\cdot X(\cdot+x_{1}))\;(\mathcal{O}_{2}(\chi\cdot X(\cdot+x_{2}))-\mathcal{O}_{2}(\chi\cdot X^{(2)}(\cdot+x_{2})))],

where we denote
X^{(i)}=X^{(i)}_{\infty}
. Thus, for any
\alpha<0
and

p\in[1,\infty]
,

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cov}(\mathcal{O}_{1}(\chi\cdot X(\cdot+x_{1})),\mathcal{O}_{2}(\chi\cdot X(\cdot+x_{2})))|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\mathcal{O}_{1},\mathcal{O}_{2}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\chi\cdot(X(\cdot+x_{1})-X^{(1)}(\cdot+x_{1}))\|_{B^{\alpha,-n}_{p,p}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{E}\left[\|\chi\cdot(X(\cdot+x_{1})-X^{(2)}(\cdot+x_{1}))\|_{B^{\alpha,-n}_{p,p}}\right].

It remains to estimate
\|\chi(X(\cdot+x_{i})-X^{(i)}(\cdot+x_{i}))\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha,-n}}
, for
i=1,2
. If
x_{1},x_{2}
are close, say
\mathfrak{l}\leqslant 8
, then we use the boundedness of the observables to conclude

|\operatorname{Cov}(\mathcal{O}_{1}(\chi\cdot X(\cdot+x_{1})),\mathcal{O}_{2}(\chi\cdot X(\cdot+x_{2})))|\lesssim 1.

If on the other hand
\mathfrak{l}>8
, then
D_{i}(1)\subset D_{i}(\mathfrak{l}/8)
and thus
\vartheta^{(i)}\equiv 1
on
\operatorname{supp}(\chi(\cdot-x_{i}))\subset D_{i}(1)
so that
(\bar{q}^{(i)})^{-1}\leqslant\mathrm{e}^{\gamma m}

on
D_{i}
. Consequently,

\|\chi\cdot X(\cdot+x_{i})-\chi\cdot X^{(i)}(\cdot+x_{i})\|_{B_{p,p,\ell}^{\alpha}}\leqslant\mathrm{e}^{m\gamma}\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(X-X^{i})\|_{L^{p}(D_{i}(1))}\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{m\gamma(1-\mathfrak{l}/8)},

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
5.1
below.
The remainder of this section will be devoted to its proof.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{l}>8
and
|\bar{\lambda}|
sufficiently small.
The solutions
X^{(i)}
to
the FBSDE (
4.1
) driven by
W^{(i)}
satisfy for some
\gamma<1
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t}\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(X_{t}-X_{t}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}\right]\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}.

Proof.

The FBSDE for the difference is given by

\displaystyle X_{t}-X_{t}^{(i)}=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\dot{G}_{s}(F_{s}(X_{s})-F_{s}(X_{s}^{(i)}))-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\dot{G}_{s}(R_{s}-R_{s}^{(i)})+W_{t}-W_{t}^{(i)},

\displaystyle R_{t}-R_{t}^{(i)}=

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s[H_{s}(X_{s})-H_{s}(X_{s}^{(i)})]-\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{D}F_{s}(X_{s})\dot{G}_{s}R_{s}-\mathrm{D}F_{s}(X_{s}^{(i)})\dot{G_{s}}R_{s}^{(i)}\right).

The estimate on the Gaussian part
W_{t}-W_{t}^{(i)}
is proven in Lemma

5.4
below. For the drift,
Z_{t}-Z_{t}^{(i)}:=(X_{t}-X_{t}^{(i)})-(W_{t}-W_{t}^{(i)})
, we apply Lemma
5.2
and
5.3
to find

\displaystyle\sup_{t}

\displaystyle\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(Z_{t}-Z_{t}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}

(5.1)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\bar{\lambda}_{s}\langle s\rangle^{-2}(\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}\|X_{s}-X_{s}^{(i)}\|_{L^{\infty,-n}(D_{i}^{c}(\mathfrak{l}/8))}+\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(X_{s}-X_{s}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\langle s\rangle^{-2}(\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}\|R_{s}-R_{s}^{(i)}\|_{L^{\infty,-n}(D_{i}^{c}(\mathfrak{l}/8))}+\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(R_{s}-R_{s}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}).

Similarly for the remainder,

\displaystyle\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(R_{t}-R_{t}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}

(5.2)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s(\langle s\rangle^{-4}\bar{\lambda}_{s}^{4}+\langle s\rangle^{-2}\bar{\lambda}_{s})(\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}\|X_{s}-X_{s}^{(i)}\|_{L^{\infty,-n}(D_{i}^{c}(\mathfrak{l}/8))}+\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(X_{s}-X_{s}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}})

\displaystyle+\int_{t}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\bar{\lambda}_{s}\langle s\rangle^{-2}(\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}\|R_{s}-R_{s}^{(i)}\|_{L^{\infty,-n}(D_{i}^{c}(\mathfrak{l}/8))}+\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(R_{s}-R_{s}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}).

In the region
D_{i}^{c}(\mathfrak{l}/8)
, we cannot expect the difference
W-W^{(i)}
to behave any better than the Brownian motion
W_{t}
itself. We
therefore control the solution in this region using the uniform bounds on
the drift from Lemma
4.1
combined with the bounds on the
Brownian motion
(W_{t})_{t}
from Lemma
4.8
. This implies for
any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\|R_{s}-R_{s}^{(i)}\|_{L^{\infty,-n}(D_{i}^{c}(\mathfrak{l}/8))}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|R_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s}\langle s\rangle^{-\varepsilon}\|X_{s}-X_{s}^{(i)}\|_{L^{\infty,-n}(D_{i}^{c}(\mathfrak{l}/8))}]

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s}\|Z_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}]+2\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s}\langle s\rangle^{-\varepsilon}\|W_{s}\|_{L^{\infty,-n}}]\lesssim 1,

where the estimate on
(W_{t})_{t}
follows from Lemma
4.8
and

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\geqslant 1}\|t^{-\varepsilon/2}W_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\geqslant 1}\|t^{-\varepsilon/2}W_{t}\|_{B_{\infty,\infty}^{\alpha,-n}}\right],\quad\text{}\alpha\in(0,\varepsilon).

Moreover, with Lemma
5.4
below,

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\geqslant 0}\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(X_{s}-X_{s}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\geqslant 0}\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(Z_{s}-Z_{s}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}\right]+\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}.

With these estimates, the equation (
5.2
) reduces to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(R_{t}-R_{t}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}+\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\geqslant 0}\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(Z_{s}-Z_{s}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}\right]

\displaystyle+\int_{t}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\bar{\lambda}_{s}\langle s\rangle^{-2}\mathbb{E}\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(R_{s}-R_{s}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}},

which implies by Gronwall’s lemma

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(R_{t}-R_{t}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}\right]\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}+\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\geqslant 0}\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(Z_{s}-Z_{s}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}\right].

Taking expectation (
5.1
) these bounds
imply

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t}\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(Z_{t}-Z_{t}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}\right]\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\bar{\lambda}_{s}\langle s\rangle^{-2+\varepsilon}\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}+\bar{\lambda}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t}\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(Z_{t}-Z_{t}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}\right],

so that for
\bar{\lambda}
sufficiently small after rearranging

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t}\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(Z_{t}-Z_{t}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}\right]\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}.

∎

Lemma 5.2
.

For any function
v
, it holds that

\|q^{(i)}\dot{G}_{s}v\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\langle s\rangle^{-2}(\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}\|v\|_{L^{\infty,-n}(D_{i}^{c}(\mathfrak{l}/8))}+\|\bar{q}^{(i)}v\|_{L^{\infty}}).

(5.3)

Proof.

First observe that since
\vartheta^{(i)}
is compactly supported, the weight

\langle x\rangle^{-n}
is uniformly bounded away from zero and thus

\vartheta^{(i)}(x)\lesssim\vartheta^{(i)}(x)\langle x\rangle^{-n}
. Combined
with the triangle inequality and the estimate (
1.11
)
on the polynomial weights, we have

\bar{q}^{(i)}(x)=\vartheta^{(i)}(x)\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m|x-x_{i}|}\lesssim\vartheta^{(i)}(x)\langle x-y\rangle^{n}\mathrm{e}^{\gamma m|x-y|}\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m|y-x_{i}|}\langle y\rangle^{-n}.

(5.4)

Thus, for any function
v
,

\displaystyle\|q^{(i)}\dot{G}_{s}v\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{x}\left|\int\mathrm{d}y\bar{q}^{(i)}(x)\dot{G}_{s}(x-y)v(y)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{x}\int\mathrm{d}y\langle x-y\rangle^{n}\mathrm{e}^{\gamma m|x-y|}\dot{G}_{s}(x-y)|\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m|y-x_{i}|}\langle y\rangle^{-n}v(y)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{x}|\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m|x-x_{i}|}\langle x\rangle^{-n}v(x)|\sup_{x}\int\mathrm{d}y\langle y-x\rangle^{n}\mathrm{e}^{\gamma m|y-x|}\dot{G}_{s}(y-x).

If
x\in D_{i}^{c}(\mathfrak{l}/8)
, then
|x-x_{i}|>\mathfrak{l}/8
so
that

\mathbbm{1}_{D_{i}^{c}(\mathfrak{l}/8)}(x)|\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m|x-x_{i}|}\langle x\rangle^{-n}v(x)|\leqslant\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}\|v(x)\|_{L^{\infty,-n}(D_{i}^{c}(\mathfrak{l}/8))}.

On the other hand, since
\mathbbm{1}_{D_{i}(\mathfrak{l}/8)}\leqslant\vartheta^{(i)}
, for
x\in D_{i}(\mathfrak{l}/8)
we can insert

\vartheta^{(i)}
to find

\mathbbm{1}_{D_{i}(\mathfrak{l}/8)}(x)|\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m|x-x_{i}|}\langle x\rangle^{-n}v(x)|\lesssim|\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m|x-x_{i}|}\vartheta^{(i)}(x)v(x)|=\|\bar{q}^{(i)}v\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Thus, the estimates on
\dot{G}
from Lemma
A.4
conclude with
the assumption that
\gamma<1
.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

In the same notation as before, the following Lipschitz
estimates apply

\displaystyle\|\bar{q}^{(i)}\dot{G}_{s}(F_{s}(\varphi)-F_{s}(\varphi^{(i)}))\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{s}\langle s\rangle^{-2}(\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}\|\varphi-\varphi^{(i)}\|_{L^{\infty,-n}(D_{i}^{c}(\mathfrak{l}/8))}+\|\bar{q}(\varphi-\varphi^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}})

(5.5)

\displaystyle\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(H_{s}(\varphi)-H_{s}(\varphi^{(i)}))\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}^{4}_{s}\langle s\rangle^{-4}(\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}\|\varphi-\varphi^{(i)}\|_{L^{\infty,-n}(D_{i}^{c}(\mathfrak{l}/8))}+\|\bar{q}(\varphi-\varphi^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}})

(5.6)

\displaystyle\|\bar{q}^{(i)}\mathrm{D}F_{s}(\varphi)\dot{G}_{s}R\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{s}\langle s\rangle^{-2}(\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}\|R\|_{L^{\infty,-n}(D_{i}^{c}(\mathfrak{l}/8))}+\|\bar{q}^{(i)}R\|_{L^{\infty}}).

(5.7)

Proof.

Regarding (
5.5
) and (
5.6
), we only show how to
commute the weight through a generic term of the Ansatz for the
force (
3.13
). The optimal, field independent bounds on

F_{s}
are then obtained in the same way as in the proof of
Proposition
3.13
. In this case, we need to estimate
expressions of the form

\sup_{x_{1}}\left|\int\mathrm{d}x_{2:\ell}f(x_{1:\ell})[\mathrm{e}^{i\beta\varphi(x_{2:\ell})}-\mathrm{e}^{i\beta\varphi^{(i)}(x_{1:\ell})}]\bar{q}^{(i)}(x_{1})\right|,

where
f
is one of the (potentially regularised) force coefficients and

x_{1:\ell}\in(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\ell}
for some
\ell
. To this end,
thanks to the boundedness of the complex exponential function, it is
sufficient to estimate the terms

\sup_{x_{1}}\left|\int\mathrm{d}x_{2:\ell}f(x_{1:\ell})|\varphi(x_{k})-\varphi^{(i)}(x_{k})|\bar{q}^{(i)}(x)\right|,\quad k=1,\ldots,\ell.

Using (
5.4
) we obtain,

\displaystyle f(x_{1:\ell})[\mathrm{e}^{i\beta\varphi(x_{2:\ell})}-\mathrm{e}^{i\beta\varphi^{(i)}(x_{1:\ell})}]\bar{q}^{(i)}(x_{1})

(5.8)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle f(x_{1:\ell})\vartheta^{(i)}(x)\langle x-x_{k}\rangle^{n}\mathrm{e}^{\gamma m|x-x_{k}|}(\varphi_{s}-\varphi_{s}^{i})(x_{k})[\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}\langle x_{k}^{-m}\rangle\mathbbm{1}_{D_{i}^{c}<(\mathfrak{l}/8)}(x_{k})

\displaystyle+\bar{q}(x_{k})\mathbbm{1}_{D_{i}(\mathfrak{l}/8)}(x_{k})].

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle[\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}\|\varphi_{s}-\varphi_{s}^{i}\|_{L^{\infty,-n}(D_{i}^{c}(\mathfrak{l}/8))}+\|(\varphi_{s}-\varphi_{s}^{i})\bar{q}\|_{L^{\infty}}]f(x_{1:\ell})\mathrm{e}^{\gamma m|x-x_{k}|}\langle x-x_{k}\rangle^{n}.

From here, thanks to the exponential decay of the force in the separation of
the points, the estimates on
f^{[\ell]}_{s}
obtained in Section

3.3
conclude since by definition of the Steiner
weights
\omega_{\varsigma}
we have for
\varsigma\in(\gamma,1)
,

\sup_{x_{1}}\left|\int\mathrm{d}x_{2:\ell}f(x_{1:\ell})\mathrm{e}^{\gamma m|x-x_{k}|}\langle x-x_{k}\rangle^{n}\right|\lesssim\interleave f\interleave.

Applying exactly the same reasoning as in the proof of Lemma
5.2

to (
5.7
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\bar{q}(x)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{D}F_{s}(X)\dot{G}_{s}R)(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{D}F_{s}(X)(x,y)\langle x-z\rangle^{n}\mathrm{e}^{\gamma m|x-y|}\int\mathrm{d}z\dot{G_{s}}(y-z)q^{(i)}(z)\langle z\rangle^{-n}R(z),

which implies the claim after splitting up the integral in between
D_{i}^{c}(\mathfrak{l}/8)
and
D_{i}(\mathfrak{l}/8)
.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

For i=1,2 and
\mathfrak{l}>8
, it holds that

\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t}\|\bar{q}^{(i)}(W_{t}-W_{t}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}]\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma m\mathfrak{l}/8}.

Proof.

First note that since
D_{i}
is compact, the restricted weight
q|_{D_{i}}
is
of order
1
in
D_{i}
, that is
1\lesssim q|_{D_{i}}\lesssim 1
. Therefore,
by Besov embeddings,

\displaystyle\|\bar{q}(W_{t}-W_{t}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}\,\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\vartheta^{(i)}(W_{t}-W_{t}^{(i)})\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\vartheta^{(i)}(W_{t}-W_{t}^{(i)})\|_{B^{\alpha-\delta}_{\infty,\infty}}\,\lesssim\,\|\vartheta^{(i)}(W_{t}-W_{t}^{(i)})\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha+\delta}},

provided
0<\delta<\alpha
and
p>d/\delta
. Following the same
logic as in the proof of Lemma
4.8
, we have for any
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
,
p\in[1,\infty)
thanks to Gaussian hypercontractivity,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t}\|\vartheta^{(i)}(W_{t}-W_{t}^{(i)})\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha}}]\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t}\|\vartheta^{(i)}(W_{t}-W_{t}^{(i)})\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha}}^{p}]^{1/p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t}\|\vartheta^{(i)}(W_{t}-W_{t}^{(i)})\|_{H^{\alpha}}^{2}]^{1/2}.

Interpolating between
L^{2}
and
H^{1}
, it is therefore sufficient to show
that

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t}\|\vartheta^{(i)}(W_{t}-W_{t}^{(i)})\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\right]\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-m\gamma\mathfrak{l}/8}.

Here, we compute similarly to the argument in Lemma
A.4
using
now the separation
d(D_{i}^{c},D_{i}(\mathfrak{l}/4))>\mathfrak{l}/4
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\vartheta^{(i)}(W_{t}-W_{t}^{(i)})\|_{{H^{1}}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{D_{i}(\mathfrak{l}/4)}\mathrm{d}x\int_{D_{i}^{c}}\mathrm{d}z(|\nabla Q_{s}(x-z)|^{2}+|Q_{s}(x-z)|^{2})

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\sup_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}[]{l}x\in D_{i}(\mathfrak{l}/4)\\
z\in D_{i}^{c}\end{array}$}}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{m}{2}\gamma|x-z|}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{D_{i}(\mathfrak{l}/4)}\mathrm{d}x\int_{D_{i}^{c}}\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{e}^{\frac{m}{2}\gamma|x-z|}(|\nabla Q_{s}(x-z)|^{2}+|Q_{s}(x-z)|^{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-m\gamma\mathfrak{l}/8}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{D_{i}(\mathfrak{l}/4)}\mathrm{d}x\int_{D_{i}^{c}}\mathrm{d}z(s|x-z|+1)\mathrm{e}^{-2s|x-z|^{2}+\frac{m}{2}\gamma|x-z|-m^{2}/s}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-m\gamma\mathfrak{l}/8}.

Finally, the maximal martingale inequalities allow to take the supremum
inside the expectation and we arrive at the claim.
∎

6
Singularity for
\beta^{2}\geqslant 4\pi

We use the FBSDE (
4.1
) to show Theorem
1.4
,
that is that the finite volume sine-Gordon measure and the Gaussian free field
are mutually singular for
\beta^{2}\geqslant 4\pi
. Our proof relies on the
asymptotics for the regularised cosine potential. It is similar in spirit to
the method used in
[
5
]
, but does not rely
on a change of measure. We also refer
to
[
33
]
, where the authors show
singularity of the
\Phi^{3}_{3}
measure using a variational problem.

Before we go into more detail, let us discuss the heuristics behind our argument. To show that absolute continuity is violated it is enough to find an event
S
such that

\mu(S)=0
but
\nu_{\operatorname{SG}}^{\rho}(S)>0
. Good candidates for events
S
are limits of
approximations of
W
and
X
, chosen such that the almost sure limits are different.
If such an event exists, then we also get the reverse singularity result for free: since
\mu(S^{c})=1
and
\nu_{\operatorname{SG}}^{\rho}(S^{c})=0
,
both measures are singular with respect to each other.

A natural choice is an appropriate rescaling of the cosine itself.
For
W
, Gaussian hypercontractivity suggests that as
t\to\infty
the martingale behaves like

\llbracket\cos(\beta W_{t}(0))\rrbracket-1=\int_{0}^{t}-\beta\llbracket\sin(\beta W_{s}(0))\rrbracket\mathrm{d}W_{s}(0)\asymp t^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}\vee 1.

For the interacting field
X_{t}=W_{t}+Z_{t}
, the Wick ordered cosine is no longer a martingale as we pick up
an additional drift term,

\displaystyle\llbracket\cos(\beta X_{t}(0))\rrbracket-1=\int_{0}^{t}-\beta\llbracket\sin(\beta X_{s}(0))\rrbracket\dot{Z}_{s}(0)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}-\beta\llbracket\sin(\beta X_{s}(0))\rrbracket\mathrm{d}W_{s}(0).

For the martingale part, we expect the same asymptotics as in the Gaussian case.
However, the drift should behave like

\int_{0}^{t}-\beta\llbracket\sin(\beta X_{s}(0))\rrbracket\dot{Z}_{s}(0)\mathrm{d}s\asymp\int_{0}^{t}\langle s\rangle^{2(1-\delta)}\langle s\rangle^{-2}\mathrm{d}s\asymp t^{1-2\delta}\vee 1.

Note that there is no blow up for
\delta>\frac{1}{2}\iff\beta^{2}<4\pi
. However,
for
\delta\leqslant\frac{1}{2}
, the drift and martingale blow up in
the ultraviolet limit at different rates and so we are able to find a rescaling of the Wick ordered
cosine which extracts only the divergence coming from the drift. In other words, we
want to find
c(t)
such that as
t\to\infty
,

c(t)\llbracket\cos(\beta W_{t}(0))\rrbracket-1\to 0,\qquad c(t)\llbracket\cos(\beta X_{t}(0))\rrbracket-1\to\infty.

To make the above heuristics precise, we have to make some adjustments.
First, instead of the pointwise cosine, we will consider an average in space.
Moreover, we will have to formulate all of the above only in terms of the terminal
values
X_{\infty}
and
W_{\infty}
correspond to the physically meaningful EQFT measures.
The bulk of this section then consists in showing that the divergence heuristics
above are precise and that the drift and the martingale can be separated by tuning

c(t)
. The result is the following theorem.

Theorem 6.1
.

For

\gamma\in\begin{cases}(1/2,1),&\delta=1/2\\
2(1/2-\delta\vee 1-3\delta,1-2\delta),&\delta\in(\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2})\end{cases},

define

r(\varepsilon)=\begin{cases}\log(\varepsilon^{-2}\vee 1)^{-\gamma},&\text{$\delta=1/2$
}\\
{\varepsilon},\,&\text{$\delta\in(\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2})$}\end{cases},

and let
\chi^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{-2}\chi(\cdot/\varepsilon)

be a standard mollifier with radially symmetric and compactly supported
Fourier transform
\hat{\chi}^{\varepsilon}=\chi(\varepsilon\cdot)
.
Define the observable

U^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)=\int r(\varepsilon)\left(\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}G^{\varepsilon}(0)}\cos(\beta(\chi^{\varepsilon}\ast\varphi)(x))-1\right)\rho(x)\mathrm{d}x,

(6.1)

where
G^{\varepsilon}=\operatorname{Cov}(\chi^{\varepsilon}\ast W_{\infty})
is
the covariance of the mollified Gaussian free field. Then, there is a
subsequence
\varepsilon_{n}\rightarrow 0
such that

|U^{\varepsilon_{n}}(Z_{\infty}+W_{\infty})|\overset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\infty\quad\text{while }\quad U^{\varepsilon_{n}}(W_{\infty})\overset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0,

(6.2)

where
Z_{\infty}=Z_{\infty}^{\rho}
solves (
4.1
).

Before we prove this statement, let us note the following consequence.

Corollary 6.2
.

For
\delta\leqslant 1/2
, that is
\beta^{2}\geqslant 4\pi
, the finite
volume sine-Gordon measure and the Gaussian free field are mutually
singular.

Proof.

For some
\alpha>0
arbitrarily small, define the event

S=\left\{\varphi\in H^{-\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{2}):\;\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}U^{\varepsilon_{n}}(\varphi)=0\right\},

where
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a suitable but fixed subsequence and
U^{\varepsilon}
is defined as in (
6.1
). It follows from
Theorem
6.1
that
\nu_{\operatorname{SG}}^{\rho}(S)=0=\mu(S^{c})
while

\mu(S)=1=\nu_{\operatorname{SG}}^{\rho}(S^{c})
, which implies the claim.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

Let
(U_{t}^{\varepsilon})_{t\geqslant 0}
be the scale interpolation of the functional

U^{\varepsilon}
such that
(U_{t}^{\varepsilon}(W_{t}))_{t}
is a martingale,
that is

U_{t}^{\varepsilon}(\varphi)=\int r(\varepsilon)\left(\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}G_{t}^{\varepsilon}(0)}\cos(\beta(\chi^{\varepsilon}\ast\varphi)(x))-1\right)\rho(x)\mathrm{d}x,

(6.3)

where
G_{t}^{\varepsilon}=\operatorname{Cov}(\chi^{\varepsilon}\ast W_{t})
. For
convenience, we always assume
\varepsilon<1
and we also write

\lambda^{\varepsilon}_{t}:=\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}G^{\varepsilon}_{t}(0)}
,
\bar{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}_{t}:=|\lambda^{\varepsilon}_{t}|
,
\varphi^{\varepsilon}=\chi^{\varepsilon}\ast\varphi
, and

W^{\varepsilon}=\chi^{\varepsilon}\ast W
. It follows from Ito’s formula
that

\displaystyle U^{\varepsilon}(Z_{\infty}+W_{\infty})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\left(\partial_{s}U^{\varepsilon}_{s}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{Tr}(\dot{G}^{\varepsilon}_{s}\mathrm{D}^{2}U^{\varepsilon}_{s})\right)(Z_{s}+W_{s})

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I}^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{D}U^{\varepsilon}_{s}(Z_{s}+W_{s})\dot{Z}_{s}

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I}\mathrm{I}^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{D}U^{\varepsilon}_{s}(Z_{s}+W_{s})\mathrm{d}W_{s}.

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I}\mathrm{I}\mathrm{I}^{\varepsilon})

Thanks to the choice of the interpolation (
6.3
), the term

(\mathrm{I}^{\varepsilon})
vanishes for all
\varepsilon
.

(\mathrm{I}\mathrm{I}\mathrm{I}^{\varepsilon})
Let for
\varphi\in\{X,W\}
,

M^{\varepsilon}_{\infty}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{D}U^{\varepsilon}_{s}(\varphi_{s})\mathrm{d}W_{s}=-\beta r(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{\infty}\bar{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}_{s}\int\mathrm{d}x\rho(x)\sin(\beta\varphi_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x))\mathrm{d}W_{s}^{\varepsilon}(x),

so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|M_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\beta^{2}r(\varepsilon)^{2}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{\infty}(\bar{\lambda}_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\int\mathrm{d}x_{1}\rho(x_{1})\int\mathrm{d}x_{2}\rho(x_{2})\sin(\beta\varphi^{\varepsilon}(x_{1}))\sin(\beta\varphi^{\varepsilon}(x_{2}))\mathrm{d}\langle W^{\varepsilon}(x_{1})W^{\varepsilon}(x_{2})\rangle_{s},

where we compute

\mathrm{d}\langle W^{\varepsilon}(x_{1})W^{\varepsilon}(x_{2})\rangle_{s}=\int\mathrm{d}y_{1}\int\mathrm{d}y_{2}\chi_{\varepsilon}(x_{1}-y_{1})\dot{G}_{s}(y_{1}-y_{2})\chi_{\varepsilon}(x_{2}-y_{2})\mathrm{d}s.

Using
\chi^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{-2}\chi(\cdot/\varepsilon)

and
\|\chi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-2(1-1/p)}

we obtain with Young’s convolution inequalities,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|M_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\beta^{2}r(\varepsilon)^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s(\bar{\lambda}_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\int\mathrm{d}x_{1}\rho(x_{1})\int\mathrm{d}x_{2}\rho(x_{2})\times

\displaystyle\times\int\mathrm{d}y_{1}\int\mathrm{d}y_{2}\chi_{\varepsilon}(x_{1}-y_{1})\dot{G}_{s}(y_{1}-y_{2})\chi_{\varepsilon}(x_{2}-y_{2})

\displaystyle\leqslant\beta^{2}r(\varepsilon)^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s(\bar{\lambda}_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\|\chi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}}\|\chi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}}\|\dot{G}_{s}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim r(\varepsilon)^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s(\bar{\lambda}_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{2}s^{-2}\mathrm{e}^{-m^{2}/s}.

Using Lemma
6.3
below,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|M_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim r(\varepsilon)^{2}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon^{-2}}\mathrm{d}ss^{-2}\mathrm{e}^{-m^{2}/s}s^{2(1-\delta)}+r(\varepsilon)^{2}\varepsilon^{-4(1-\delta)}\int_{\varepsilon^{-2}}^{\infty}s^{-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim r(\varepsilon)^{2}\begin{cases}\log(\varepsilon^{-2}\vee 1)+1,&\delta=1/2,\\
\varepsilon^{-2+4\delta}+1&\delta<1/2.\end{cases}

Combined, choosing
\gamma>1/2
implies for
\delta=1/2
,

\mathbb{E}|M_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\lesssim r(\varepsilon)^{2}(\log(\varepsilon^{-2})+1)=\log(\varepsilon^{-2})^{-2\gamma}(\log(\varepsilon^{-2})+1)\overset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}0.

Otherwise, if
\delta<1/2
,

\mathbb{E}|M_{\infty}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2\gamma-2+4\delta}\overset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow}0,

provided
\gamma>2(1/2-\delta)
. Passing to a subsequence, this
implies
M^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{\infty}\rightarrow 0
almost surely and
consequently also
\sup_{n}|M^{\varepsilon_{n}}|<\infty
almost surely.
For the Gaussian, we can set
Z\equiv 0
to find

U^{\varepsilon_{n}}(W_{\infty})=M_{\infty}^{\varepsilon_{n}}
, which
gives the second claim in (
6.2
).

(\mathrm{I}\mathrm{I}^{\varepsilon})
To get started, we split this term
into the two parts,

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I}\mathrm{I}^{\varepsilon})=

\displaystyle-\beta r(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\int\mathrm{d}x\rho(x)\sin(\beta\varphi^{\varepsilon}(x))\chi_{\varepsilon}\ast\dot{G}_{s}F_{s}^{[1]}(\varphi_{s})

\displaystyle-\beta r(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\lambda_{s}^{\varepsilon}\int\mathrm{d}x\rho(x)\sin(\beta\varphi^{\varepsilon}(x))\chi_{\varepsilon}\ast\dot{G}_{s}[(F_{s}-F_{s}^{[1]})(\varphi_{s})+R_{s}]

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I}\mathrm{I}^{\varepsilon}_{1})+(\mathrm{I}\mathrm{I}_{\geqslant 2}^{\varepsilon}).

(\mathrm{I}\mathrm{I}^{\varepsilon}_{\geqslant 2})
We claim that under
the assumptions of Theorem
6.1
, this term is uniformly
bounded in
\varepsilon>0
and
\Omega
. Indeed, using again the estimate
on
\lambda^{\varepsilon}_{s}
from Lemma
6.3
, we compute with
Proposition
3.13
and the a priori estimates on the
remainder (Proposition
4.1
),

\displaystyle(\mathrm{I}\mathrm{I}_{\geqslant 2}^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\leqslant r(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\bar{\lambda}_{s}^{\varepsilon}\left|\beta\int\mathrm{d}x\rho(x)\sin(\beta\varphi^{\varepsilon}(x))(\chi_{\varepsilon}\ast\dot{G}_{s})[(F_{s}-F_{s}^{[1]})(\varphi_{s})+R_{s}]\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim r(\varepsilon)\|\chi_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\bar{\lambda}_{s}^{\varepsilon}\|\dot{G}_{s}[(F_{s}-F_{s}^{[1]})(\varphi_{s})+R_{s}]\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim r(\varepsilon)\left[1+\int_{1}^{\varepsilon^{-2}\vee 1}\mathrm{d}s\langle s\rangle^{(1-\delta)}\langle s\rangle^{-2}\langle s\rangle^{1-2\delta}\right]+r(\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{-2(1-\delta)}\int_{\varepsilon^{-2}}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\langle s\rangle^{-2}\langle s\rangle^{1-2\delta}.

\displaystyle\lesssim r(\varepsilon)((\langle s\rangle^{1-3\delta})|_{s=1}^{s\equiv\varepsilon^{-2}}+1)+r(\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{-2+6\delta}

\displaystyle\lesssim r(\varepsilon)(\varepsilon^{-2+6\delta}+1)+r(\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{-2+6\delta}.

For
\delta=1/2
, we see that
-2+6\delta=1>0
so that

\sup_{\varepsilon>0}(\mathrm{I}\mathrm{I}^{\varepsilon}_{\geqslant 2})<\infty
.
For
\delta<1/2
, the assumption
\gamma>2(1-3\delta)
implies the
analogous bound.

(\mathrm{I}\mathrm{I}^{\varepsilon}_{1})
We show that for
\gamma
small
enough according to the assumptions, this term can be split into a divergent
term, and uniformly bounded almost surely finite term. To get started note
that

U^{\varepsilon}_{t}(\varphi)=\frac{\lambda_{t}^{\varepsilon}}{2}\int\rho(x)\left(\sum_{\sigma=\pm 1}(\mathrm{e}^{i\sigma\beta\varphi^{\varepsilon}(x)}-1)\right)\mathrm{d}x,

so that in the same way as in Section
3
, we find

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}U_{t}(\varphi_{t})\dot{G}_{t}F^{[1]}_{t}(\varphi_{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\{\pm 1\}}\int\mathrm{d}x_{1}\rho(x_{1})\int\mathrm{d}x_{2}\lambda_{t}^{\varepsilon}\lambda_{t}\mathrm{e}^{i\beta(\sigma_{1}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x_{1})+\sigma_{2}\varphi_{t}(x_{2}))}\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\beta^{2}(\chi^{\varepsilon}\ast\dot{G}_{t})(x_{1}-x_{2}).

Motivated by the renormalisation constant produced by the neutral
contribution (c.f. Section
3.4
), we treat the summands for
the charged case,

\mathcal{C}^{\varepsilon}_{t}=-\sum_{\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}\in\{\pm 1\}}\int\mathrm{d}x_{1}\rho(x_{1})\int\mathrm{d}x_{2}\lambda_{t}^{\varepsilon}\lambda_{t}\mathrm{e}^{i\beta(\sigma_{1}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x_{1})+\sigma_{2}\varphi_{t}(x_{2}))}\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\beta^{2}(\chi^{\varepsilon}\ast\dot{G}_{t})(x_{1}-x_{2}),

and the neutral case

\mathcal{N}^{\varepsilon}_{t}=-\sum_{\sigma_{1}=-\sigma_{2}\in\{\pm 1\}}\int\mathrm{d}x_{1}\rho(x_{1})\int\mathrm{d}x_{2}\lambda_{t}^{\varepsilon}\lambda_{t}\mathrm{e}^{i\beta(\sigma_{1}\varphi^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x_{1})+\sigma_{2}\varphi_{t}(x_{2}))}\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\beta^{2}(\chi^{\varepsilon}\ast\dot{G}_{t})(x_{1}-x_{2}),

separately.

(
\mathcal{C}^{\varepsilon}_{t}
).
We start by rewriting this sum
again as a trigonometric function,

\mathcal{C}^{\varepsilon}_{t}=r(\varepsilon)\int\mathrm{d}z\beta^{2}(\chi^{\varepsilon}\ast\dot{G}_{t})(z)\lambda_{t}\lambda_{t}^{\varepsilon}\int\mathrm{d}x\rho(x)\cos(\beta(\varphi_{t}(z-x)+\varphi_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x))),

where we can add and subtract
\varphi_{t}(x)
to obtain using the
trigonometric identities,

\displaystyle\cos(\beta(\varphi_{t}(z-x)+\varphi_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)))=

\displaystyle\cos(\beta(\varphi_{t}(z-x)+\varphi_{t}(x)))\cos(\beta(\varphi_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)-\varphi_{t}(x)))

\displaystyle-\sin(\beta(\varphi_{t}(z-x)+\varphi_{t}(x)))\sin(\beta(\varphi_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)-\varphi_{t}(x))).

Since both of these terms are estimated in the exact same way, let us only
consider the contribution coming from the cosine. Here, we apply the
trigonometric identities again, now for
\varphi_{t}=Z_{t}+W_{t}
, to rewrite

\displaystyle\cos(\beta(\varphi_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)-\varphi_{t}(x)))=

\displaystyle\cos(\beta(Z_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)-Z_{t}(x)))\cos(\beta(W_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)-W_{t}(x)))

\displaystyle-\sin(\beta(Z_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)-Z_{t}(x)))\sin(\beta(W_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)-W_{t}(x))).

Use the trivial estimate
|\cos(\beta(W_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)-W_{t}(x)))|\leqslant 1
for the contribution from the GFF while we use the additional
regularity
\sup_{t}\|Z_{t}\|_{B_{\infty,\infty}^{2\delta-}}<\infty

(see Theorem
4.6
) in the drift
Z_{t}
to get the
improved bound

\sup_{x}|\cos(\beta(Z_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)-Z_{t}(x)))|\lesssim\varepsilon^{{}_{1}}\|Z_{t}\|_{B^{{}_{2}}_{\infty,\infty}},\quad\text{provided }{}_{1}<2\delta.

It remains to deal with
\int\mathrm{d}x\rho(x)\cos(\beta(\varphi_{t}(z-x)+\varphi_{t}(x)))
, for which we follow the same procedure,

\displaystyle\cos(\beta(\varphi_{t}(z-x)+\varphi_{t}(x)))=

\displaystyle\cos(\beta(Z_{t}(z-x)+Z_{t}(x)))\cos(\beta(W_{t}(z-x)+W_{t}(x)))

\displaystyle-\sin(\beta(Z_{t}(z-x)+Z_{t}(x)))\sin(\beta(W_{t}(z-x)+W_{t}(x))),

so that for any
s>0
,

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}x\rho(x)\llbracket\cos(\beta(\varphi_{t}(z-x)+\varphi_{t}(x)))\rrbracket

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\cos(\beta(Z_{t}(z-\cdot)+Z_{t}(\cdot)))\|_{B^{s}_{p,p}}\|\llbracket\cos(\beta(W_{t}(z-\cdot)+W_{t}(\cdot)))\rrbracket\rho(\cdot)\|_{B^{-s}_{q,q}}

\displaystyle+\|\sin(\beta(Z_{t}(z-\cdot)+Z_{t}(\cdot)))\|_{B^{s}_{p,p}}\|\llbracket\sin(\beta(W_{t}(z-\cdot)+W_{t}(\cdot)))\rrbracket\rho(\cdot)\|_{B^{-s}_{q,q}}

Here, we defined the Wick ordered cosine with respect to the Gaussian
W_{t}(z-x)\pm W_{t}(x)
in the usual way,

\llbracket\cos(\beta(\varphi_{t}(z-x)\pm\varphi_{t}(x)))\rrbracket:=\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}[|W_{t}(z-x)\pm W_{t}(x)|^{2}]}\cos(\beta(\varphi_{t}(z-x)\pm\varphi_{t}(x))),

(6.4)

and similarly for the sine. In what follows we will focus on the cosine term.
The sine can be dealt with in the same way.
It follows from Lemma
6.5
below that for any

{}_{1}>0
,
{}_{3}>2-3\delta
and
\alpha<2\delta
sufficiently
close to
2\delta
,

\sup_{z,\varepsilon}\mathrm{e}^{-\beta^{2}G_{t}(z)}t^{-{}_{3}}|z|^{{}_{1}/2}\|\llbracket\cos(\beta(W_{t}(z-\cdot)+W_{t}(\cdot)))\rrbracket\rho(\cdot)\|_{B^{-\alpha}_{q,q}}<\infty\quad a.s.

Moreover, from Theorem
4.6
,
\sup_{t}\|\cos(\beta(Z_{t}(z-\cdot)+Z_{t}(\cdot)))\|_{B^{\alpha}_{p,p}}\lesssim 1
for any
\alpha<2\delta
so that by a Kolmogorov argument,

\mathbb{M}^{c}:=\sup_{z,\varepsilon}|z|^{{}_{1}}\mathrm{e}^{-\beta^{2}G_{t}(z)}t^{-{}_{3}}\int\mathrm{d}x\rho(x)\llbracket\cos(\beta(\varphi_{t}(z-x)+\varphi_{t}(x)))\rrbracket<\infty\quad a.s.

Combined, this implies

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}^{\varepsilon}_{t}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left|r(\varepsilon)\int\mathrm{d}z\beta^{2}(\chi^{\varepsilon}\ast\dot{G}_{t})(z)\bar{\lambda}_{t}\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{\varepsilon}\int\mathrm{d}x\rho(x)\cos(\beta(\varphi_{t}(z-x)+\varphi_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)))\right|
