\displaystyle\|\dot{G}_{t}u\|_{L^{p}(w)}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-1-1/p}\|u\|_{L^{1}(w)}.

Moreover, with
C_{t}:=(G_{\infty}-G_{t})
it holds for any
s\geqslant t
and
c>0
sufficiently small, and
\alpha>(1-2\delta)\vee 0=\left(\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}-1\right)\vee 0
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\dot{G}_{s}(x)\mathrm{e}^{\beta^{2}C_{s}(x)}|x|^{2\alpha}\mathrm{e}^{ct|x|^{2}}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\langle s\rangle^{-2-\alpha}.

(A.6)

Proof.

For the first estimate we simply compute from (
2.4
) and Lemma

A.2
,

\|Q_{t}\|_{L^{p}(w)}^{p}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{e}^{-pm^{2}/2s}\mathrm{e}^{-2pt|x|^{2}}|x|^{2\alpha}w(x)\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-1-\alpha}.

In the polynomial case, the second bound is now a simple consequence of
Young’s convolution inequality and (
1.11
). For the
case of the exponential weights, observe that by the triangle inequality,

\|Q_{s}u_{s}\|_{L^{p}(w)}^{p}\leqslant\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}x\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{e}^{\gamma m|x-y|}Q_{s}(x-y)\mathrm{e}^{\gamma m|y|}u_{s}(y)\right)^{p}\leqslant\|Q_{s}\|^{p}_{L^{1}(w)}\|u_{s}\|_{L^{p}(w)}^{p},

which again implies the claim with Lemma
A.2
. The
estimates on
\dot{G}_{t}
follow from the estimates on
Q_{t}
the convolution
inequalities, since

\|\dot{G}_{t}\|_{L^{p}(w)}=\|Q_{t}\ast Q_{t}\|_{L^{p}(w)}\leqslant\|Q_{t}\|_{L^{1}}\|Q_{t}\|_{L^{p}(w)}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-1-1/p}.

For the estimate (
A.6
), we start from
Lemma
A.1
and the definition of
\dot{G}_{s}
, to
estimate

\dot{G}_{s}(x)\mathrm{e}^{\beta^{2}C_{s}(x)}\lesssim s^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-m^{2}/s}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{s}{4}|x|^{2}}|x|^{-\beta^{2}/2\pi}\langle s\rangle^{-\beta^{2}/4\pi}.

Moreover, for
s\geqslant t
and
c,\tilde{c}>0
sufficiently small
(more precisely,
c\in\left(0,\frac{1}{4}\right)
and
\tilde{c}\in\left(0,\frac{1}{4}-c\right)
), we have
\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{s}{4}|x|^{2}}\mathrm{e}^{ct|x|^{2}}\leqslant\mathrm{e}^{-\tilde{c}s|x|^{2}}
. Combining
both observations we see that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\dot{G}_{s}(x)\mathrm{e}^{\beta^{2}C_{s}(x)}|x|^{2\alpha}\mathrm{e}^{ct|x|^{2}}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim\int|x|^{2(\alpha-\beta^{2}/4\pi)}\mathrm{e}^{-\tilde{c}s|x|^{2}}\mathrm{e}^{-m^{2}/s}s^{-1-\beta^{2}/4\pi}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{-\beta^{2}/4\pi}\mathrm{e}^{-m^{2}/s}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{2(\alpha-\beta^{2}/4\pi)+1}\mathrm{e}^{-\tilde{c}sr^{2}}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle s\rangle^{-2-\alpha},

provided that
r\mapsto r^{2(\alpha-\beta^{2}/4\pi)+1}\mathrm{e}^{-\tilde{c}sr^{2}}
is integrable over
\mathbb{R}_{+}
, which is exactly the
condition
\alpha>\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}-1
.
∎

Lemma A.5
.

For any
\alpha\in[0,1]
,
k\in\mathbb{R}

and
p\in[1,\infty]
, we have

\left\|\int_{0}^{T}Q_{s}u_{s}\mathrm{d}s\right\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha,k}}\lesssim\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|\langle s\rangle^{\alpha/2+\varepsilon}u_{s}\|_{L^{p,k}}.

(A.7)

Moreover, in
L^{2}
the improved bound

\left\|\int_{0}^{T}Q_{s}u_{s}\mathrm{d}s\right\|_{H^{1,k}}^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|u_{s}\|_{L^{2,k}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s.

(A.8)

holds.

Proof.

For any
\bar{\varepsilon}>0
and
\bar{p}>p
, it holds

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{0}^{t}Q_{s}u_{s}\mathrm{d}s\right\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha,k}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\|\int_{0}^{t}Q_{s}u_{s}\mathrm{d}s\right\|_{B_{\bar{p},\bar{p}}^{\alpha,k}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\|Q_{s}\|_{B_{1,p}^{\alpha,n}}\|u_{s}\|_{L^{p,k}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sup_{s}\|\langle s\rangle^{\alpha/2+\varepsilon}u_{s}\|_{L^{p,k}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\langle s\rangle^{-\alpha/2-\varepsilon}\|Q_{s}\|_{B_{1,p}^{\alpha,n}}.

Moreover, by the interpolation of Besov spaces, for any
p>p(\alpha)=(1-\alpha)^{-1}
and
\tilde{\varepsilon}>0
sufficiently small,

\|Q_{s}\|_{B_{1,p}^{\alpha,n}}\leqslant\|Q_{s}\|_{B_{1,p(\alpha)}^{\alpha,n}}\lesssim\|Q_{s}\|_{L^{1,n}}^{1-\alpha}\|Q_{s}\|^{\alpha}_{B_{1,\infty}^{1-\tilde{\varepsilon},n}}\lesssim\langle s\rangle^{-1+\alpha}\|Q_{s}\|_{B^{1-\tilde{\varepsilon},n}_{1,\infty}}^{\alpha},

so that the claim will follow once we compute

\|Q_{s}\|_{B^{1-\tilde{\varepsilon},n}_{1,\infty}}\lesssim\sup_{|y|\leqslant 1}\int\mathrm{d}x\frac{|Q_{s}(x-y)-Q_{s}(x)|}{|y|}\langle x\rangle^{n}\lesssim\langle s\rangle^{-1/2}.

But this follows from a simple Taylor expansion,

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}x|Q_{s}(x-y)-Q_{s}(x)|

\displaystyle=|y|s\mathrm{e}^{-m^{2}/2s}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\int\mathrm{d}x\langle x\rangle^{n}|x-\vartheta y|\mathrm{e}^{-2s|x-\vartheta y|^{2}}

\displaystyle=|y|s\mathrm{e}^{-m^{2}/2s}\int\mathrm{d}x|x|\mathrm{e}^{-s|x|^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|y|s^{-1/2}\mathrm{e}^{-m^{2}/2s}.

To remove the
\varepsilon
in the
L^{2}
estimates, we pass to the Fourier
transform and use the fact that
Q_{s}
is diagonal in Fourier space. Since
w(x):=\langle x\rangle^{k}
grows at most polynomially, we have
w\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. For this computation only, we denote the
Fourier transform of a distribution
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
by

\hat{f}=\mathcal{F}(f)
. Repeatedly applying Hölder’s inequality and
Parseval’s identity yields after some manipulation,

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}sQ_{s}u_{s}\right\|_{H^{\alpha}(w)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\mathcal{F}(w(1-\Delta)^{\alpha/2}Q_{s}u_{s})\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int\mathrm{d}k\hat{w}(\xi-k)(1+|k|^{2})^{\alpha/2}s\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{m^{2}+|k|^{2}}{2s}}\hat{u}(k)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathrm{d}\xi)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}k(1+|k|^{2})^{\alpha/2}\hat{w}(\xi-k)\left(\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\frac{1}{s^{1+\alpha}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{m^{2}+|k|^{2}}{2s}}\right)^{1/2}\times\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\times\left.\left(\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{e}^{-m^{2}/2s}s^{-1+\alpha}\hat{u}_{s}^{2}(k)\right)^{1/2}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathrm{d}\xi)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}k\frac{(1+|k|^{2})^{\alpha/2}}{(|k|^{2}+m^{2})^{\alpha/2}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{m^{2}+|k|^{2}}{2t}}\hat{w}(\xi-k)\left(\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\langle s\rangle^{-1+\alpha}\hat{u}_{s}^{2}(k)\right)^{1/2}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathrm{d}\xi)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{k}\frac{(1+|k|^{2})^{\alpha}}{(|k|^{2}+m^{2})^{\alpha}}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{d}k\hat{w}(\xi-k)\left(\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\langle s\rangle^{-1+\alpha}\hat{u}_{s}^{2}(k)\right)^{1/2}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathrm{d}\xi)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\langle s\rangle^{-1+\alpha}\|\hat{w}\ast\hat{u}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\langle s\rangle^{-1+\alpha}\|u_{s}\|_{L^{2}(w)}^{2}.

∎

Remark A.6
.

Lemma
A.5
takes advantage of the concrete choice for
the scale interpolation to get the optimal regularity estimates
(
A.8
) in
L^{2}
. For a general scale interpolation, not
necessarily diagonal in Fourier space, we have to use (
A.7
) and
give up an arbitrarily small
\varepsilon>0
in regularity. This is not
crucial to the analysis, but would in general lead to slightly worse
results, e.g. replacing
\mathbb{D}=L^{2,n}
by
\mathbb{D}=H^{\varepsilon,n}
in the infinite volume variational problem in Theorem

7.7
.

A.2
Proof of
Lemma
2.1

We first restrict ourselves to the case
p\in[1,\infty)
. To this end, we
use the translation invariance of the Law of
W_{t}
, and hypercontractivity to
estimate

\mathbb{E}\|W_{t}\|_{B^{-\varepsilon,-n}_{p,p}}^{p}=\sum_{i\geqslant-1}2^{-i\varepsilon p}\int\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}W_{t}(x)|^{p}]\langle x\rangle^{-pn}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\sum_{i\geqslant-1}2^{-i\varepsilon p}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}W_{t}(0)|^{2}]^{p/2}.

(A.9)

Since
\Delta_{i}W_{t}(0)=\langle W_{t},K_{i}\rangle
and
\operatorname{Cov}(W_{t})=G_{t}=(m^{2}-\Delta)^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-(m^{2}-\Delta)/t}
, we can compute the
expectation on the right hand side as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}W_{t}(0)|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\langle W_{t},K_{i}\rangle\langle W_{t},K_{i}\rangle]

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}\xi\frac{|\varphi_{i}(\xi)|^{2}}{m^{2}+|\xi|^{2}}\mathrm{e}^{-(m^{2}-\Delta)/t}

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{R_{1}2^{i}}^{R_{2}2^{i}}\frac{r}{(m^{2}+r^{2})}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(2^{i}).

Here we used the fact that
\varphi_{i}
is supported on an annulus with radii

R_{1}2^{i}
,
R_{2}2^{i}
in the second to last estimate. Inserting this bound in
(
A.9
) yields the claim for
p\in[1,\infty)
. For
p=\infty
, we use the Besov embedding
\|\cdot\|_{B^{-\alpha,-n}_{\infty,\infty}}\lesssim\|\cdot\|_{B^{-\alpha+\varepsilon}_{p,p}}
for
p>2/\varepsilon
.

Finally, applying exactly the same reasoning to the increment
W_{\infty}-W_{t}
instead shows the convergence in
L^{p}(\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P},B^{-\varepsilon,-n}_{p,p})
for any
p\in[1,\infty)
.

A.3
Proof of
Lemma
3.4

Suppose for concreteness that
q(\xi_{1:\ell})>0
and recall that we want
to show

\Gamma_{t,s}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{\ell})\leqslant\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}(G_{t}(0)-G_{s}(0))+C.

We assume that (possibly after relabelling),

\sigma_{k}=\begin{cases}+1,&k\leqslant q,\\
(-1)^{k}&k>q,\end{cases}

and that
\xi_{1}
is the most isolated charge, that is
\min_{k>q}|x_{1}-x_{k}|=\max_{j\leqslant q}\min_{k>q}|x_{j}-x_{k}|
.
We split the matrix into the 3 components

\Gamma_{t,s}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{n})=\Gamma_{t,s}(\xi_{1})+\Gamma_{t,s}(\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{n})+\sigma_{1}\sum_{i>1}\sigma_{i}(G_{t}-G_{s})(x_{1}-x_{i}).

(A.10)

By the definition of
\Gamma
and the basic heat kernel estimates
(
A.1
), the first summand is

\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}(G_{t}-G_{s})(0)\leqslant\frac{\beta^{2}}{8\pi}(\log(t\vee 1)-\log(s\vee 1))+C.

The second summand is bounded from above by (
3.22
).
So (
3.23
) will follow once we establish an upper bound
for the last term in (
A.10
). Towards this goal, we start
by extracting the charged and neutral part,

\sum_{i>1}\sigma_{1}\sigma_{i}(G_{t}-G_{s})(x_{1}-x_{i})=\sum_{i=2}^{q}\sigma_{1}\sigma_{i}(G_{t}-G_{s})(x_{1}-x_{i})+\sum_{i=q+1}^{\ell}\sigma_{1}\sigma_{i}(G_{t}-G_{s})(x_{1}-x_{i}).

The first (charged) part satisfies
\sigma_{1}\sigma_{i}=1
and we can use the
same reasoning as in (
3.22
) to conclude boundedness from the
positivity of
G
. The second (neutral) part also contains contributions with
the “bad” signs
\sigma_{1}\sigma_{i}=-1
and requires special attention.
Since this part is neutral, we know that the sum contains an even number of
points and we can proceed by considering the neutral pairs
(x_{q+2i},x_{q+2i+1})
,
i=0,1,\ldots
one at a time. In other words, the claim will
follow if there is a constant
C>0
such that for any
(y,z)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
,

(G_{t}-G_{s})(x_{1}-y)-(G_{t}-G_{s})(x_{1}-z)\leqslant C.

(A.11)

By construction, one of the terms in these pairings comes with the “good”
sign, which we are going to use to bound the neutral contribution. We start by
rewriting the covariance using the kernel representation,

\displaystyle(G_{t}-G_{s})(x_{1}-y)-(G_{t}-G_{s})(x_{1}-z)=-\int_{t}^{s}\mathrm{d}\tau[\dot{G}_{\tau}(x_{1}-y)-\dot{G}_{\tau}(x_{1}-z)]

\displaystyle=-\int_{t}^{s}\mathrm{d}\tau\tau^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-m^{2}/\tau}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\tau}{4}|x_{1}-y|^{2}}-\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\tau}{4}|x_{1}-z|^{2}}\right).

If the charged edge is the shortest edge in the triangle connecting
x_{1},y,z
, that is
|x_{1}-y|\leqslant|x_{1}-z|
, then

\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\tau}{4}|x_{1}-y|^{2}}-\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\tau}{4}|x_{1}-z|^{2}}\geqslant 0,

and we can bound (
A.11
) with
C=0
. Otherwise, one of the
neutral edges
|x_{1}-z|
or
|z-y|
is the shortest edge. Thanks to the choice of
x_{1}
as the most isolated charge,
|y-z|\leqslant|x_{1}-z|
. If
|y-z|=0
, then
(G_{t}-G_{s})(x_{1}-y)-(G_{t}-G_{s})(x_{1}-z)=0
and (
A.11
) is trivially true. Thus,
we may assume that all edges have positive lengths. On
\tau>|y-z|^{-2}
, we directly compute

\int^{s}_{|z-y|^{-2}}\mathrm{d}\tau\tau^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-m^{2}/r}\left|\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\tau}{4}|x_{1}-y|^{2}}-\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\tau}{4}|x_{1}-z|^{2}}\right|\leqslant 2\int^{s}_{|z-y|^{-2}}\mathrm{d}\tau\tau^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-m^{2}/\tau}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{\tau}{4}|z-y|^{2}}\lesssim 1.

On
\tau\leqslant|y-z|^{-2}
, we use (
A.4
) combined with
the translation invariance of
\dot{G}
to conclude

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{|z-y|^{-2}}\mathrm{d}\tau[\dot{G}_{\tau}(x_{1}-y)-\dot{G}_{\tau}(x_{1}-z)]

\displaystyle\lesssim|y-z|\int_{t}^{|y-z|^{-2}}\mathrm{d}\tau|\nabla\dot{G_{\tau}}(x_{1}-y+\tau(y-z))|

\displaystyle\lesssim|y-z|\int_{t}^{|y-z|^{-2}}\mathrm{d}\tau\langle\tau\rangle^{-1/2}\lesssim 1,

which completes the proof of Lemma
3.4
.

A.4
Proof of Lemma
4.8

We first show the claim for
p<\infty
. To this
end, we start by rewriting
t^{-\alpha/2}W_{t}
for
t\geqslant 1
using
Ito’s formula

t^{-\alpha/2}W_{t}=W_{1}-\frac{\alpha}{2}\int_{1}^{t}s^{-1-\alpha/2}W_{s}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{1}^{t}s^{-\alpha/2}\mathrm{d}W_{s},

so that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{t}\|t^{-\alpha/2}W_{t}\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha-\varepsilon,-n}}\lesssim&\|W_{1}\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha-\varepsilon,-n}}+\int_{1}^{\infty}s^{-1-\alpha/2}\|W_{s}\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha-\varepsilon,-n}}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\sup_{t}\left\|\int_{1}^{t}s^{-\alpha/2}\mathrm{d}W_{s}\right\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha-\varepsilon,-n}}.\end{split}

(A.12)

For the bounded variation part, a similar computation to the proof of Lemma

2.1
shows that for any
\tilde{\varepsilon}>0
we have

\mathbb{E}\|W_{s}\|_{B^{\alpha-\varepsilon}_{p,p}}\lesssim\langle s\rangle^{\alpha/2-(\varepsilon-\tilde{\varepsilon})/2}.

Therefore, choosing
\tilde{\varepsilon}\in(0,\varepsilon)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{1}^{\infty}s^{-1-\alpha/2}\|W_{s}\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha,-n}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{1}^{\infty}s^{-1-\tilde{\varepsilon}/2}\mathbb{E}[\langle s\rangle^{\alpha/2-(\varepsilon-\tilde{\varepsilon})/2}\|W_{s}\|_{B^{\alpha-\varepsilon}_{p,p}}]\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{1}^{\infty}s^{-1-\tilde{\varepsilon}/2}<\infty.

Regarding the martingale
M_{t}=\int_{1}^{t}s^{-\varepsilon/2}\mathrm{d}W_{s}
, we
compute by translation invariance, the maximal inequalities and Gaussian
hypercontractivity,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t}\|M_{t}\|^{p}_{B^{\alpha,-n}_{p,p}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t}\sum_{i\geqslant-1}2^{i(\alpha-\varepsilon)p}\int|\Delta_{i}M_{t}(x)|^{p}\langle x\rangle^{-pn}\mathrm{d}x\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i\geqslant-1}2^{i(\alpha-\varepsilon)p}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t}|\Delta_{i}M_{t}(0)|^{p}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i\geqslant-1}2^{i(\alpha-\varepsilon)p}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}M_{\infty}(0)|^{p}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i\geqslant-1}2^{i(\alpha-\varepsilon)p}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}M_{\infty}(0)|^{2}]^{p/2}.

The covariance of
M_{\infty}
can be computed directly, as for some constant

C
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\langle M_{\infty},f\rangle\langle M_{\infty},g\rangle]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\int\mathrm{d}xf(x)\int\mathrm{d}ys^{-\alpha}\dot{G}_{s}(x-y)g(y)

\displaystyle=C\int\mathrm{d}x\int\mathrm{d}yf(x)((m^{2}-\Delta)^{-1-\alpha}g)(x)

\displaystyle=C\langle f,(m^{2}-\Delta)^{-1-\alpha}g\rangle.

Since
\Delta_{i}M_{\infty}(0)=\langle M_{\infty},K_{i}\rangle
, we have

\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}M_{\infty}(0)|^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[|\langle M_{\infty},K_{i}\rangle|^{2}]=C\langle K_{i},(m^{2}-\Delta)^{-1-\alpha}K_{i}\rangle=\int\mathrm{d}\xi\frac{|\varphi_{i}(\xi)|^{2}}{(m^{2}+|\xi|^{2})^{1+\alpha}},

where we used
K_{i}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\varphi_{i})
. Since
\varphi
is
radially symmetric and supported on an annulus with radii
{R_{1}}
,
R_{2}
,
we see passing to spherical coordinates using
\varphi_{i}=\varphi(2^{-i}\cdot)

\int\mathrm{d}\xi\frac{|\varphi_{i}(\xi)|^{2}}{(m^{2}+|\xi|^{2})^{1+\alpha}}\lesssim\int_{R_{1}2^{i}}^{R_{2}2^{i}}\frac{r^{d-1}}{(m^{2}+r^{2})^{1+\alpha}}\mathrm{d}r\overset{d=2}{\lesssim}(R_{1}2^{i})^{-2\alpha}\lesssim 2^{-2\alpha i}.

Therefore, for
0<\alpha<\varepsilon
,

\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t}\|M_{t}\|^{p}_{B^{(\alpha-\varepsilon),-n}_{p,p}}]\lesssim\sum_{i\geqslant-1}2^{i(\alpha-\varepsilon)p}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}M_{\infty}(0)|^{2}]^{p/2}\lesssim\sum_{i\geqslant-1}2^{-pi\varepsilon}<\infty,

and inserting the bounds in the (
A.12
)

\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t}\|t^{-\alpha/2}W_{t}\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha-\varepsilon,-n}}]\lesssim 1+\mathbb{E}\int_{1}^{\infty}s^{-1-\alpha/2}\|W_{s}\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha-\varepsilon,-n}}\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t\geqslant 1}\|M_{t}\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha-\varepsilon,-n}}]<\infty.

Finally, for the case
p=\infty
, we use the Besov embedding
\|\cdot\|_{B^{\alpha-\gamma,-n}_{\infty,\infty}}\lesssim\|\cdot\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha,-n}}
for
\alpha-\gamma>0
and
p>2/\gamma
. Then
choosing
\gamma\in(0,\varepsilon)
and
p
sufficiently large it holds
that

\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t\geqslant 1}\|t^{-\alpha/2}W_{t}\|_{B_{\infty,\infty}^{\alpha-\varepsilon,-n}}]\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t\geqslant 1}\|t^{-\alpha/2}W_{t}\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha-(\varepsilon-\gamma),-n}}^{p}]^{1/p}\lesssim 1.

Appendix B
Auxiliary estimates on the Fourier coefficients

We collect some additional estimates on the kernels
f
defined
in (
3.16
). No new ideas are needed for these estimates and
we only want to briefly illustrate how the proofs in the previous section can
be modified to obtain the additional results.

B.1
Dependence on the terminal condition

The following Lemma quantifies the dependence on the terminal
condition (
3.3
) and is used to show convergence as
the small-scale cut-off is removed in Proposition
3.13
.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
T_{1},T_{2}>0
. For
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small
depending only on
\delta
, it holds for all
t<\infty
,

\interleave f^{[\ell],T_{1}}_{t}-f_{t}^{[\ell],T_{2}}\interleave_{t}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{\ell}\langle t\rangle^{-(\ell-1)+2\varepsilon}\langle T_{1}\wedge T_{2}\rangle^{-\varepsilon}.

(B.1)

Proof.

We proceed inductively (mod the procedure required to remove the
regularising kernel for
\ell\geqslant 3
) starting from
\ell=1
. For
simplicity, let us assume that
T_{2}\leqslant T_{1}
, then

|f^{[1],T_{1}}_{t}-f_{t}^{[1],T_{2}}|=|\mathbbm{1}_{\{t\geqslant T_{2}\}}(f_{t}^{[\ell],T_{1}}-f_{T_{2}}^{[\ell],T_{2}})|=\mathbbm{1}_{\{t\geqslant T_{2}\}}\lambda_{t}\left(1-\frac{\lambda_{T_{2}}}{\lambda_{t}}\right).

And using that
G_{t}-G_{T_{2}}\geqslant 0
on
\{t\geqslant T_{2}\}
,

0\leqslant 1-\frac{\lambda_{T_{2}}}{\lambda_{t}}=1-\mathrm{e}^{-\beta^{2}(G_{t}-G_{T_{2}})(0)}\leqslant\beta^{2}(G_{t}-G_{T_{2}})(0)\leqslant\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}\log(t\vee 1)+C.

Therefore,

\interleave f^{[1],T_{1}}_{t}-f_{t}^{[1],T_{2}}\interleave_{t}=|f^{[1],T_{1}}_{t}-f_{t}^{[1],T_{2}}|\lesssim\mathbbm{1}_{\{t\geqslant T_{2}\}}\bar{\lambda}_{t}\log(t\vee 1)\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}t^{2\varepsilon}\langle T_{2}\rangle^{-\varepsilon},

as required.
Moving on now to
\ell\geqslant 2
, assuming that
(
B.1
) holds for
\ell^{\prime}<\ell
, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{T}f^{[\ell]}_{t}=

\displaystyle\delta_{T}f_{T_{2}}^{[\ell]}-\sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}[]{c}I_{1}\dot{\cup}I_{2}=[\ell]\\
k\in\{1,2\}\end{array}$}}}\sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}[]{c}i\in I_{1}\\
j\in I_{2}\end{array}$}}}C(|I_{1}|,|I_{2}|)\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}\times

\displaystyle\times\int_{t\vee T_{2}}^{T_{2}}\delta_{T}f_{s}(\xi_{I_{1}})f_{s}^{[T_{k}]}(\xi_{I_{2}})\mathrm{e}^{\Gamma_{t,s}(\xi_{I})}\dot{G}_{s}(x_{i}-x_{j})\mathrm{d}s.

Inserting the bounds on
\delta_{T}f^{[\ell^{\prime}]}
for
\ell^{\prime}<\ell
, and the
bounds on
\interleave f_{t}^{[\ell^{\prime}]}\interleave_{t}
, we find in the same way
as in section
3
,

\interleave\int_{t}^{T_{2}}\delta_{T}f_{s}(\xi_{I_{1}})f_{s}(\xi_{I_{2}})\mathrm{e}^{\Gamma_{t,s}(\xi_{I})}\dot{G}_{s}(x_{i}-x_{j})\mathrm{d}s\interleave_{t}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{\ell}t^{-(\ell-1)+2\varepsilon}\langle T_{2}\rangle^{-\varepsilon}.

Regarding the remaining term, for
\ell>1
, it holds for
t\leqslant T_{2}
,

\displaystyle\interleave

\displaystyle\delta_{T}f_{T_{2}}^{[\ell]}\interleave_{t}

\displaystyle=\interleave f_{T_{2}}^{[\ell],T_{1}}\interleave_{t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{I_{1}\dot{\cup}I_{2}=[\ell]}\int_{T_{2}}^{T_{1}}\interleave f_{s}^{T_{1}}(\xi_{I_{1}})\interleave_{s}\interleave f_{s}^{T_{1}}(\xi_{I_{2}})\interleave_{s}\times

\displaystyle\times\interleave\sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}[]{c}i\in I_{1}\\
j\in I_{2}\end{array}$}}}\mathrm{e}^{\Gamma_{t,s}(\xi_{I})}\frac{\kappa_{t}(\xi_{I})\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}}{\kappa_{s}(\xi_{I_{1}})\kappa_{s}(\xi_{I_{2}})}\dot{G}_{s}(x_{i}-x_{j})\interleave\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}t^{1/2}\bar{\lambda}_{T_{2}}^{2}\langle T_{2}\rangle^{-3/2},&\ell=2,q(\xi_{I})=0\\
\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{1}\bar{\lambda}_{T_{2}}^{\ell-1}\langle T_{2}\rangle^{-(\ell-1)},&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{\ell}\langle t\rangle^{-(\ell-1)+2\varepsilon}\langle T_{2}\rangle^{-\varepsilon},

as required.
∎

B.2
Dependence on the mollification

Suppose that
(\eta^{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
is an approximation to
the identity on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that for
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}G^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x):=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int\mathrm{d}y\eta^{\varepsilon}(x-y)G_{t}(y)=G_{t}(x).

Define the truncated solution to the flow equation
F^{\varepsilon}
, its
Fourier coefficients
f^{\varepsilon}
and the renormalisation constants

\lambda^{\varepsilon}
in the same way as before, with
G
replaced by

G^{\varepsilon}
. To prove reflection positivity in
Section
8
, we have to understand the dependency of the flow
equation on this mollification.
The required argument is essentially the same as for the dependence on
T
in
the previous section, with the main difference being that we do not need to
distinguish cases
t\geq T_{1}\wedge T_{2}
and
t\leqslant T_{1}\wedge T_{2}
.

Lemma B.2
.

If
\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{\varepsilon}\leqslant C\bar{\lambda}_{t}
for some constant
C>0
, then there is a subsequence

\varepsilon_{N}\rightarrow 0
such that

\interleave f^{[\ell]}_{t}-f_{t}^{[\ell],\varepsilon_{N}}\interleave\lesssim N^{-1}\interleave f_{t}^{[\ell]}\interleave.

Proof.

Let us first derive the dependency of the renormalisation constant

\lambda^{\varepsilon}
on
\varepsilon
. Since
\bar{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}_{t}\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}
uniformly in
\varepsilon>0
,

|\lambda_{t}^{\varepsilon}-\lambda_{t}|=\left|\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}G_{t}^{\varepsilon}(0)}-\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}G_{t}(0)}\right|\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}G_{t}(0)}|G_{t}^{\varepsilon}(0)-G_{t}(0)|.

Choosing
(\varepsilon_{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
|G^{\varepsilon_{N}}_{t}(0)-G_{t}(0)|\leqslant N^{-1}
, this implies with
the definition of
\lambda_{t}
,

|\lambda_{t}^{\varepsilon}-\lambda_{t}|\lesssim N^{-1}\bar{\lambda}_{t},

As a by-product, since
f^{[1]}_{t}=-\frac{\beta\lambda_{t}}{2i}
, this
shows the claim for
\ell=1
. For
\ell=2,3
we proceed as in the
bounds derived in Lemma
3.6
and

3.7
. For example, for the charged case

\displaystyle\interleave f^{[2](\pm 2)}_{t}-f_{t}^{[2],(\pm 2),\varepsilon}\interleave

\displaystyle=C\sup_{x_{1}}\left|\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\int\mathrm{d}x_{2}[(\lambda_{s}^{\varepsilon})^{2}\dot{G^{\varepsilon}_{s}}(x_{1}-x_{2})\mathrm{e}^{\Gamma_{t,s}^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})}-(\lambda_{s})^{2}\dot{G}_{s}(x_{1}-x_{2})\mathrm{e}^{\Gamma_{t,s}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})}]\right|

\displaystyle=C\left|\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\int\mathrm{d}x_{2}[\lambda_{t}^{\varepsilon}\lambda_{s}^{\varepsilon}\dot{G^{\varepsilon}_{s}}(x_{2})-\lambda_{t}\lambda_{s}\dot{G}_{s}(x_{2})]\right|

\displaystyle=C\left|\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s\left[\lambda_{t}^{\varepsilon}\lambda_{s}^{\varepsilon}\int\mathrm{d}x_{2}\left(\dot{G^{\varepsilon}_{s}}(x_{2})-\dot{G_{s}}\left(x_{2}\right)\right)-(\lambda_{t}\lambda_{s}-\lambda_{t}^{\varepsilon}\lambda_{s}^{\varepsilon})\int\mathrm{d}x_{2}\dot{G}_{s}(x_{2})\right]\right|.

But thanks to the translation invariance,

\int\mathrm{d}x_{2}(\dot{G^{\varepsilon}_{s}}(x_{2})-\dot{G_{s}}(x_{2}))=\int\mathrm{d}x_{1}\eta^{\varepsilon}(x_{1})\int\mathrm{d}x_{2}(\dot{G}_{s}(x_{1}-x_{2})-\dot{G}_{s}(x_{2}))=0.

Thus, with the estimates on
|\lambda_{s}^{\varepsilon}-\lambda_{s}|\lesssim N^{-1}\bar{\lambda}_{s}
and
|\lambda_{t}\lambda_{s}-\lambda_{t}^{\varepsilon}\lambda_{s}^{\varepsilon}|\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}\bar{\lambda}_{s}N^{-1}

\interleave f^{[2](\pm 2)}_{t}-f_{t}^{[2](\pm 2),\varepsilon_{N}}\interleave\lesssim\int_{t}^{T}\mathrm{d}s|\lambda_{t}\lambda_{s}-\lambda_{t}^{\varepsilon}\lambda_{s}^{\varepsilon}|\int\mathrm{d}x_{2}\dot{G}_{s}(x_{2})\lesssim\bar{\lambda}_{t}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1}N^{-1}.

The remaining bounds on the neutral part
f^{[2](0)}
and
f^{[3]}
follow
in the same way.
∎

Appendix C
Wick-ordered cosine

For the large deviations principle in Section
7.3
, we rely on the
convergence of the Wick-ordered sine and cosine in the first region
\beta^{2}<4\pi
.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
\beta^{2}\in[0,4\pi)
. For any
p\in[1,\infty)
and
\alpha>\beta^{2}/4\pi
, it holds that

\sup_{T}\mathbb{E}\|\llbracket\cos(\beta W_{T})\rrbracket-1\|_{B_{p,p}^{-\alpha}(\langle x\rangle^{-n})}^{p}\lesssim\beta^{2}.

Moreover, as
T\rightarrow\infty
, the martingale
(\llbracket\cos(\beta W_{T})\rrbracket)
converges in
L^{p}(\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P};B_{p,p}^{-\alpha}(\langle x\rangle^{-n})
and almost surely. We denote the unique limit
by
\llbracket\cos(\beta W_{\infty})\rrbracket
. An analogous statement
holds for the Wick-ordered sine.

The main ingredient in for the proof of Lemma
C.1
is the
following point-wise estimate on the quadratic variation.

Lemma C.2
.

Let
N_{t}=\llbracket\cos(\beta W_{t})\rrbracket
and
\beta^{2}\in[0,4\pi)
. Then, its quadratic variation
satisfies for any
\varepsilon>0
,

|\langle\Delta_{i}N\rangle_{t}(x)|\lesssim\beta^{2}2^{2i(\beta^{2}/4\pi+\varepsilon)}.

The analogous statement holds for the cosine replaced by the sine.

Proof.

Expanding the Wick-ordered cosine with Ito’s formula we find,

\llbracket\cos(\beta W_{t})\rrbracket=1-\beta\int_{0}^{t}\llbracket\sin(\beta W_{s})\rrbracket\mathrm{d}W_{s}=1-\beta\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}G_{s}(0)}\sin(\beta W_{s})\mathrm{d}W_{s}.

Therefore, using
\operatorname{Cov}(W_{s})=G_{s}
and applying Young’s inequality
repeatedly,

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta_{i}N\rangle_{t}(x)|

(C.1)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\beta^{2}\int^{t}_{0}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{e}^{\beta^{2}G_{s}(0)}\int\mathrm{d}y_{1}K_{i}(x-y_{1})\int\mathrm{d}y_{2}K_{i}(x-y_{2})\times

\displaystyle\times\sin(\beta W_{s}(y_{1}))\sin(\beta W_{s}(y_{2}))\mathrm{d}\langle W(y_{1}),W(y_{2})\rangle_{s}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\beta^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{e}^{\beta^{2}G_{s}(0)}\sup_{x}\int\mathrm{d}y_{1}K_{i}(x-y_{1})\int\mathrm{d}y_{2}K_{i}(x-y_{2})\dot{G}_{s}(y_{1}-y_{2})

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\beta^{2}\|K_{i}\|_{L^{1}}\|K_{i}\|_{L^{p}}\sup_{y_{1}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{e}^{\beta^{2}G_{s}(0)}\|\dot{G}_{s}(y_{1}-y_{2})\|_{L^{q}(\mathrm{d}y_{2})}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\beta^{2}\|K_{i}\|_{L^{1}}\|K_{i}\|_{L^{p}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{e}^{\beta^{2}G_{s}(0)}\|\dot{G}_{s}\|_{L^{q}},

where
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=1
are to be determined later. So using
the estimates on the heat kernel

\|\dot{G_{s}}\|_{L^{q}}\lesssim\langle s\rangle^{-1}\|Q_{s}\|_{L^{q}}\lesssim\langle s\rangle^{-1-1/q},

combined with the estimates on the Littlewood–Paley kernels
(
1.12
), the previous computation (
C.1
) and

\mathrm{e}^{\beta^{2}G_{s}(0)}\lesssim\langle s\rangle^{\beta^{2}/4\pi}
from
Lemma
A.1
gives

|\langle\Delta_{i}N\rangle_{t}(x)|\lesssim\beta^{2}2^{2i\frac{p-1}{p}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\langle s\rangle^{\beta^{2}/4\pi}\langle s\rangle^{-1-1/q}.

For the integral to be bounded uniformly in
t
, we need
\frac{1}{q}>\beta^{2}/4\pi
and since
p,q
are Hölder conjugates this means

\frac{p-1}{p}=\frac{1}{q}>\beta^{2}/4\pi.

Consequently, we can choose
p,q\in[1,\infty)
if and only if
\beta^{2}<4\pi
which gives the claim.
∎

Proof of Lemma
C.1
.

Recall the definition of the Besov norms,

\mathbb{E}[\|N_{t}\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha}(\langle x\rangle^{-n})}^{p}]=\sum_{i\geqslant-1}2^{-ip\alpha}\mathbb{E}\|\Delta_{i}N_{t}\|_{L^{p,-n}}^{p}.

(C.2)

We compute by Lemma
C.2
and the
Burkholder–Davis–Gundy’s inequalities, for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\mathbb{E}\|\Delta_{i}N_{t}\|_{L^{p}(\langle x\rangle^{-n})}^{p}\lesssim\int\mathrm{d}x\langle x\rangle^{-pn}\mathbb{E}[|\langle\Delta_{i}\llbracket\cos(\beta W)\rrbracket(x)\rangle_{t}|]^{p/2}\lesssim\beta^{2}2^{pi(\beta^{2}/4\pi+\varepsilon)}.

Therefore, (
C.2
) is finite provided
\beta^{2}/4\pi<\alpha

and the convergence now follows from the martingale convergence theorem.
∎

We also owe the proof of Lemma
6.5
.

Proof of Lemma
6.5
.

(N)
Let

M_{t}(x)=\llbracket\cos(\beta(W_{t}(z-x)-W_{t}(x)))\rrbracket:=\llbracket\cos(\beta(\delta_{z}W_{t}(x)))\rrbracket,

which by Ito’s formula can be written as

\displaystyle M_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}-\beta\llbracket\sin(\beta\delta_{z}W_{s}(x))\rrbracket\mathrm{d}(\delta_{z}W_{s}(x))

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}-\beta\llbracket\sin(\beta\delta_{z}W_{s}(x))\rrbracket\int\mathrm{d}y(Q_{s}(x-z-y)-Q_{s}(x-y))\mathrm{d}B_{s}(y),

where we recall that
(B_{t})_{t}
is a cylindrical Brownian motion on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. Now

Q_{s}(x-z-y)-Q_{s}(x-y)=|z|\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\vartheta\nabla Q_{s}(x-y-\vartheta z),

so that by translation invariance and since
\rho
has compact support, we conclude
\mathbb{E}\|M_{t}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\mathbb{E}[|M_{t}(0)|^{2}]^{1/2}
.
The latter can be estimates as follows

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|M_{t}(0)|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\langle M(0),M(0)\rangle_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta^{2}|z|^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\llbracket\sin(\beta\delta_{z}W_{s}(0))\rrbracket\llbracket\sin(\beta\delta_{z}W_{s}(0))\rrbracket\times

\displaystyle\times\int\mathrm{d}y\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\vartheta_{1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\vartheta_{2}\nabla Q_{s}(y-\vartheta_{1}z)\nabla Q_{s}(y-\vartheta_{2}z)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\beta^{2}|z|^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{e}^{2\beta^{2}G_{s}(0)-2\beta^{2}G_{s}(z)}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\vartheta_{1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\vartheta_{2}\int\mathrm{d}y\nabla Q_{s}(y-(\vartheta_{1}-\vartheta_{2})z)\nabla Q_{s}(y).

Using the estimates from Lemma
A.4
,
\|\nabla^{\alpha}Q_{s}\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\langle s\rangle^{-1/p+\alpha/2}
so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\mathrm{e}^{\beta^{2}G_{t}(z)}M_{t}(0)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\beta^{2}|z|^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{e}^{2\beta^{2}G_{s}(0)}\mathrm{e}^{2\beta^{2}(G_{t}-G_{s})(z)}\times

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\vartheta_{1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\vartheta_{2}\int\mathrm{d}y\nabla Q_{s}(y-(\vartheta_{1}-\vartheta_{2})z)\nabla Q_{s}(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim|z|^{2-8(1-\delta)}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\vartheta_{1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\vartheta_{2}\int\mathrm{d}y\nabla Q_{s}(y-(\vartheta_{1}-\vartheta_{2})z)\nabla Q_{s}(y)

\displaystyle\leqslant|z|^{2-8(1-\delta)}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\|\nabla Q_{s}\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla Q_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|z|^{2-8(1-\delta)}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\langle s\rangle^{-1/2}\langle s\rangle^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim|z|^{2-8(1-\delta)}t.

Using the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
4.8
in the
scales as well as the usual Kolmogorov argument for the
L^{\infty}
-norm in

z
we obtain for any
{}_{1}>1/2
,
{}_{2}>-1+4(1-\delta)

\mathbb{E}[\sup_{t,z}t^{-{}_{1}}|z|^{{}_{2}}\mathrm{e}^{\beta^{2}G_{t}(z)}\|M_{t}\|_{L^{1}}]\lesssim 1,

and thus

\sup_{t,z}t^{-{}_{1}}|z|^{{}_{2}}\mathrm{e}^{\beta^{2}G_{t}(z)}\|M_{t}\|_{L^{1}}<\infty,\quad a.s.

(C)
We start by estimating

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\llbracket\cos(\beta(W_{t}(\cdot-z))+W_{t}(\cdot))\rrbracket\rho(\cdot)\|_{B^{-s}_{p,p}(\mathrm{d}x)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\geqslant-1}2^{-isp}\mathbb{E}\|\Delta_{i}\llbracket\cos(\beta(W_{t}(\cdot-z)+W_{t}(\cdot)))\rrbracket\|^{p}_{L^{p}(\mathrm{d}x)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\rho}\sum_{i\geqslant-1}2^{-isp}\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}\llbracket\cos(\beta(W_{t}(\cdot-z)+W_{t}(\cdot)))\rrbracket(0)|^{2}]^{p/2},

where we again used that
\rho
is smooth and compactly supported and that
the law of
W
is translation invariant. Here, the Littlewood-Paley blocks
act only in
x
, that is

\Delta_{i}\llbracket\cos(\beta(W_{t}(\cdot-z)+W_{t}(\cdot)))\rrbracket(x)=\int\mathrm{d}yK_{i}(x-y)\llbracket\cos(\beta(W_{t}(y-z)+W_{t}(y)))\rrbracket.

Developing the martingale
\llbracket\cos(\beta(W_{t}(y-z)+W_{t}(y)))\rrbracket
along the scales with Ito’s formula, we obtain,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{i}\llbracket\cos(\beta(W_{t}(z)+W_{t}(0)))\rrbracket|^{2}]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\beta^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int\mathrm{d}y_{1}\int\mathrm{d}y_{2}K_{i}(y_{1})K_{i}(y_{2})\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}[|W_{s}(y_{1}-z)+W_{s}(y_{1})|^{2}]}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}[|W_{s}(y_{2}-z)+W_{s}(y_{2})|^{2}]}_{s}

\displaystyle\times\mathrm{d}\langle W(y_{1}-z)+W(y_{1}),W(y_{2}-z)+W(y_{2})\rangle_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int\mathrm{d}y_{1}\int\mathrm{d}y_{2}K_{i}(y_{1})K_{i}(y_{2})\mathrm{e}^{2\beta^{2}G_{s}(z)}\mathrm{e}^{2\beta^{2}G_{s}(0)}[\dot{G}_{s}(y_{1}-y_{2})+\dot{G}_{s}(y_{1}-y_{2}-z)]\mathrm{d}s.

Thanks to the positivity of
G
, we have the estimate
\mathrm{e}^{2\beta^{2}(G_{s}-G_{t})(z)}\leqslant 1
for
t\geqslant s
so that for any
1/r+1/q=1
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int\mathrm{d}y_{1}\int\mathrm{d}y_{2}K_{i}(y_{1})K_{i}(y_{2})\mathrm{e}^{2\beta^{2}(G_{s}-G_{t})(z)}\mathrm{e}^{2\beta^{2}G_{s}(0)}[\dot{G}_{s}(y_{1}-y_{2})+\dot{G}_{s}(y_{1}-y_{2}-z)]\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int\mathrm{d}y_{1}\int\mathrm{d}y_{2}K_{i}(y_{1})K_{i}(y_{2})\mathrm{e}^{2\beta^{2}G_{s}(0)}[\dot{G}_{s}(y_{1}-y_{2})+\dot{G}_{s}(y_{1}-y_{2}-z)]\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\|K_{i}\|_{L^{1}}\|K_{i}\|_{L^{r}}\int_{0}^{t}\langle s\rangle^{4(1-\delta)}\|\dot{G_{s}}\|_{L^{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{2i/q}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\langle s\rangle^{-1-1/q+4(1-\delta)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(t^{-1/q+4(1-\delta)}\vee 1)2^{2i/q}.

Therefore,

\mathbb{E}\|\mathrm{e}^{-\beta^{2}G_{t}(z)}\llbracket\cos(\beta(W_{t}(\cdot-z)+W_{t}(\cdot)))\rrbracket\rho(\cdot)\|^{p}_{B^{-s}_{p,p}(\mathrm{d}x)}\lesssim(t^{-1/2q+2(1-\delta)}\vee 1)^{p}\sum_{i\geqslant-1}2^{-ip\left(s-\frac{1}{q}\right)}.

Using the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
4.8
, we can
choose
1/2q
sufficiently close to
s\in(0,2\delta)
sufficiently
large to conclude for any
{}_{1}>0
,
{}_{2}>2-3\delta
,

\sup_{t,z}\|\mathrm{e}^{-\beta^{2}G_{t}(z)}|z|^{{}_{1}/2}t^{-{}_{2}}\llbracket\cos(\beta(W_{t}(\cdot-z)+W_{t}(\cdot)))\rrbracket\rho(\cdot)\|_{B^{-s}_{p,p}(\mathrm{d}x)}<\infty,\quad a.s.

∎
